ASTRA — DEFERRED WORK
=====================================================================
Things named in the project plan that are NOT yet built, and why.
Updated as each slice completes. Last updated: 2026-08-31.

Legend:
  [BLOCKED]  waits on a later phase that must come first
  [SCOPED]   deliberately out of scope for now, cheap to add later
  [GAP]      should exist, simply not written yet
  [KNOWN]    built but with a stated limitation
  [PARTIAL]  usable implementation exists, but hardening or scale work remains


---------------------------------------------------------------------
PHASE 2 — ASTRONOMY DATA LAYER
---------------------------------------------------------------------


[PARTIAL] Gaia epoch photometry
          Only the DR3 main table (static astrometry + mean photometry)
          is queried. Gaia's time-series photometry for variables is a
          separate, much larger table.
          Gaia DR4, expected 2026-12-02, is the eventual fix: it publishes
          epoch time series (astrometry, photometry, BP/RP and RVS spectra)
          for ALL sources rather than DR3's ~12M-source subset — ~400 TB
          total, against DR3's ~10 TB. That is what would let Gaia become a
          real time-domain survey and make gaia_only / gaia_tess / all_three
          answerable the same way the ztf_gaia entry in Phase 8 now is for
          Gaia-as-static-context. Deliberately not planned around here: it
          is ~3.5 months out from this writing and enormous in volume; see
          the ztf_gaia entry for what is answerable today without it.
          UPDATE 2026-08-19: GaiaEpochAdapter validates offline DR4 fixtures
          (source_id/time/flux/error), records rejection reasons, and exposes
          the expected release gate. Live DR4 ingestion remains disabled until
          the archive release and schema are verified.


[PARTIAL] Future survey connectors
          The registry now carries explicit capabilities, resolution,
          credential, and enabled-by-default metadata. Bounded SDSS spectrum
          metadata, Pan-STARRS DR2 mean-photometry, and Chandra Source
          Catalog (CSC) X-ray metadata adapters are registered as opt-in
          connectors; default acquisition remains limited to ZTF/Gaia/TESS
          until provider contracts are validated.
          UPDATE 2026-08-19: all three metadata-only connectors
          (`sdss.py`, `panstarrs.py`, `chandra.py`) now have fixture tests
          (`tests/test_surveys_sdss.py`, `test_surveys_panstarrs.py`,
          `test_surveys_chandra.py`) covering row parsing, malformed/missing
          fields, capability declarations, and the `fetch_light_curves` no-op
          contract, mocking `astra.netclient.get`. A previous version of this
          entry claimed such tests already existed for SDSS/Pan-STARRS; they
          did not (checked: only `tests/test_surveys_base.py` existed) — this
          is now fixed rather than just corrected.
          UPDATE 2026-08-19 (2): Swift (SXPS point-source catalog) and
          XMM-Newton (4XMM-DR13 serendipitous source catalog) adapters
          (`swift.py`, `xmm.py`) now exist, registered opt-in with the same
          metadata-only shape, each with its own fixture test file
          (`tests/test_surveys_swift.py`, `test_surveys_xmm.py`).
          Chandra/Swift/XMM's `cone_search` implementations target plausible
          endpoint shapes for each archive's cone-search service; none has
          been validated against the real, live service yet (exact field
          names, auth requirements, real rate limits) — only the parsing/
          registration contract and malformed-response handling are tested
          against fixture payloads. This is a live-contract validation gap,
          not a missing capability: run each connector against its real
          endpoint once network access to validate is available, and adjust
          field-name fallbacks/rate limits as needed.
          UPDATE 2026-08-28: SDSS and Pan-STARRS DR2 (`sdss.py`,
          `panstarrs.py`) flipped to `enabled_by_default = True` -- both are
          anonymous/no-auth endpoints, already rate-limited in
          `netclient.py` (`"sdss": 0.2s`, `"mast": 0.2s`), and their
          docstrings record live-session verification beyond fixture tests.
          Chandra (`chandra.py`) stays opt-in: its `cone_search` targets
          `cda.cfa.harvard.edu/csccli/browse`, confirmed live to require an
          undocumented `packageset` parameter (400 without it, 404 for every
          guessed value tried) -- a genuine open contract gap, not yet a
          provider-validated connector. Chandra's separate
          `query_band_fluxes` (VizieR `IX/70`) IS live-verified, but
          acquisition depends on `cone_search`, not that function, so the
          registry-wide default stays False for Chandra pending a real fix
          to the `packageset` value.
          UPDATE 2026-08-19 (3): DES, Hubble, and JWST adapters
          (`des.py`, `hubble.py`, `jwst.py`) now exist, registered opt-in
          with the same metadata-only shape, each with its own fixture test
          file (`tests/test_surveys_des.py`, `test_surveys_hubble.py`,
          `test_surveys_jwst.py`; 33 tests). DES DR2 goes through NOIRLab
          Astro Data Lab's anonymous TAP endpoint
          (https://datalab.noirlab.edu/tap/sync) with fixed, ASTRA-
          parameterised ADQL against `des_dr2.main` — no user SQL reaches
          the service. Hubble and JWST share one MAST CAOM helper
          (`_mast_caom.py`) hitting `Mast.Caom.Filtered.Position` with a
          server-side `obs_collection` filter, so the 200-row cap is spent
          on the requested mission. A `datalab` entry was added to
          `netclient.REQUEST_INTERVAL_SECONDS` at 1.0s because a TAP sync
          query is a database job rather than a REST lookup; HST/JWST reuse
          the existing `mast` bucket.
          These three carry the SAME live-contract validation gap as
          Chandra/Swift/XMM above: the ADQL column names for `des_dr2.main`
          and the CAOM field names come from published documentation, not
          from a live call. Nothing here has been run against the real
          endpoints. The per-row try/except means a field-name mismatch
          degrades to "zero sources returned" rather than a crash, but a
          silent zero is exactly what a fixture test cannot distinguish from
          a correct empty cone — validate against the live services before
          trusting a negative result from any of them.
          Rubin/LSST still needs an isolated adapter, and is additionally
          blocked on credentials rather than on effort: its TAP endpoint
          (https://data.lsst.cloud/api/tap) requires a data-rights account
          token, so it cannot be anonymously reached or fixture-validated
          the way the others were. It would have to ship as a
          bring-your-own-credentials connector (`credential_required = True`,
          the pattern TNS already needs).
          LIGO/Virgo/KAGRA and CHIME are not point-source cone-search
          surveys and do not fit the current `SurveyConnector` interface
          as-is; they need a base-class/interface design decision before an
          adapter can be written, not just an implementation. Both do have
          usable public APIs (GWOSC at https://gwosc.org/api/, no auth,
          JSON; CHIME/FRB open data + VOEvent service), so the blocker is
          genuinely the interface: they publish *events* with sky-
          localization regions, not sources at a point.
          UPDATE 2026-08-20: ALeRCE alert-broker adapter (`alerce.py`) now
          exists, registered opt-in, unblocking real Rubin/LSST photometry
          through the broker route rather than the credential-gated direct
          TAP path described above. ALeRCE is one of NOIRLab's official
          community brokers for LSST (which began its 10-year survey on
          2026-06-30 and now streams ~7M alerts/night), alongside ANTARES/
          Fink/Lasair, and its REST API (https://api.alerce.online/) is
          confirmed genuinely public and unauthenticated -- `credential_
          required = False`, the first LSST-adjacent connector able to say
          so. Unlike the nine metadata-only connectors above, it implements
          a real `fetch_light_curves()`: ALeRCE serves actual ZTF/LSST
          detections (mjd/magpsf/sigmapsf/fid or band), not just catalogue
          metadata, making it ASTRA's first new time-domain light-curve
          source since TESS/QLP. A single `release` kwarg ("ztf" or "lsst",
          default "ztf") selects the underlying survey, mirroring the
          ALeRCE Python client's own `query_objects(survey=...)` parameter
          and the same "one constructor kwarg drives a lookup table"
          mechanism `tess.py`'s `author` uses; `resolution_arcsec` is set
          per-release (1.0" for ZTF, 0.2" for LSST) since seeing-limited
          LSST is meaningfully sharper. ZTF's numeric `fid` (1/2/3 -> g/r/i)
          and LSST's own lettered bands (u/g/r/i/z/y) are both mapped
          inside `alerce.py`, not `base.py`, per this module's own "adding
          a survey means adding a connector" rule.
          Same live-contract-validation gap as every connector above,
          arguably wider here: the exact REST endpoint paths and JSON field
          names (oid/meanra/meandec/classifier/probability for cone search;
          mjd/magpsf/sigmapsf/fid for detections) come from ALeRCE's
          documentation and the official Python client's method signature,
          not a live authenticated fetch -- a direct check this session
          confirmed `https://api.alerce.online/ztf/v1/` resolves as a real,
          live API host and that `query_objects(ra=, dec=, radius=,
          survey=)` needs no auth, but the exact response JSON shape was
          not observed. One further explicit, unvalidated assumption: LSST
          queries are requested by passing `survey="lsst"` to that same
          confirmed `/ztf/v1/objects`-prefixed endpoint, rather than a
          hypothetical separate `/lsst/v1/` path that was never confirmed
          to resolve. Run against the real endpoint before trusting a
          negative cone-search result, exactly as instructed for Chandra/
          Swift/XMM/DES/Hubble/JWST above. The direct Rubin/LSST TAP path
          (https://data.lsst.cloud/api/tap) remains blocked on a
          data-rights credential as before; this entry adds a working
          credential-free alternative alongside it, it does not remove
          that blocker.
          Tests: `tests/test_surveys_alerce.py`, same fixture-mocked
          `netclient.get` shape as the other nine connectors, plus
          light-curve-specific coverage (band mapping for both releases,
          multi-band grouping, malformed-detection-row handling) the
          metadata-only connectors don't need.
          UPDATE 2026-08-21: the Rubin/LSST alert path is hardened without
          building a direct TAP connector. `alerts.poll()` now accepts an
          optional `params` override merged into the real request (e.g.
          `alerts.poll("alerce", params={"survey": "lsst"})`), so the
          existing credential-free `alerce` provider entry can be polled for
          LSST-labelled alerts through the one existing endpoint rather than
          adding a second `DEFAULT_ENDPOINTS` key -- no new transport, same
          dedup/cursor machinery. Two metrics this backlog card names were
          previously unmeasured anywhere in the codebase and are now part of
          every `alerts.poll()` response: `duplicate_rate` (derived from the
          dedup bookkeeping `_stable_packet_id`/`_ingest_outcome` already
          did, just not reported as a rate) and `latency_summary` (median/
          p95/n of `received_utc - event_time` in seconds, computed via
          `association._parse_time` reused rather than a third ISO/JD
          parser). `surveys/alerce.py` gained `photometric_residual()`, a
          per-band comparison of ALeRCE-reported magnitudes against a
          caller-supplied reference (typically built from
          `crossmatch.match_catalogs()` against stored Gaia/PS1 rows) --
          diagnostic evidence only, not a correction applied to the stored
          curve, and explicitly not wired into `scoring.WEIGHTS`.
          `tests/test_surveys_alerce.py` gained a schema-contract test class
          that encodes ALeRCE's DOCUMENTED `/objects`/`/detections` field
          contract as its own fixture (independent of the current parsing
          code), so a future silent field-name drift in the real service
          shows up as a fixture/contract mismatch to fix, not a silent pass.
          The credential-storage half of a future direct Rubin/LSST TAP
          connector is now built and reusable: `credentials.py`'s
          TNS-specific DPAPI helpers (`save_tns_credentials` etc.) are now
          thin wrappers over generic `save_credentials(name, payload)` /
          `load_credentials(name)` / `credential_status(name)` /
          `clear_credentials(name)`, keyed by provider name and verified
          byte-identical for TNS's own on-disk file/entropy/description
          (`tests/test_credentials.py`). `RubinTAPConnector` itself
          (direct `data.lsst.cloud/api/tap` access) remains DELIBERATELY
          DEFERRED, not forgotten: ALeRCE already delivers real,
          credential-free LSST photometry today, and a direct-TAP connector
          could only ever be exercised against mocked responses until ASTRA
          has an actual Rubin data-rights account token in hand -- building
          it now would be speculative effort with no way to validate it
          against the live service, the same live-contract-validation gap
          every other connector in this file already carries, just with no
          way to even attempt validation yet. When that credential exists,
          `tap.py`'s hardcoded `provider="datalab"` string and lack of an
          auth-header parameter on `query()` are the two things that would
          need to change first; the credential module above is ready.

[PARTIAL] Moving-object tracklet linking and orbit determination  <-- NEW 2026-08-22
          `moving_objects.py` is a `catalogs.py`/`gw.py`-shaped module, not a
          `SurveyConnector` -- a tracklet is a time-ordered sequence of
          positions, the same reason GW/FRB events don't fit `cone_search`
          either. Two live-endpoint checks were made before writing it, the
          same discipline `gw.py`/`frb.py` document:
            1. The Minor Planet Center DOES publish a genuine, credential-
               free query API for orbital elements --
               `minorplanetcenter.net/web_service/search_orbits`, with
               shared, publicly documented basic-auth credentials, not a
               per-user key. This corrects an earlier planning assumption
               that MPC's only interface was one-way ADES/obs80 submission.
               `mpc_search_orbits()` uses it for orbital-element residual
               comparison.
            2. That MPC endpoint does NOT expose raw per-night detections or
               tracklets, only already-linked, already-fitted elements.
               ATLAS's public forced-photometry service
               (fallingstar-data.com/forcedphot/) requires sign-in and
               returns light curves at supplied/ephemeris positions, not a
               raw detection stream, even for its documented "moving objects
               by MPC name" mode. ZTF/Pan-STARRS moving-object detections are
               a separate product neither `surveys/ztf.py` nor
               `surveys/panstarrs.py` ingests today. No credential-free, real
               multi-survey tracklet source exists to wire up yet -- this is
               a genuine, not a glossed-over, gap.
          Built and validated against synthetic ground truth (recoverable by
          construction, same discipline `artifact.calibrate_from_injection`
          uses): `assemble_tracklets()` groups same-night detections and
          accepts a group only when a straight-line RA/Dec-vs-time fit's
          residual clears a tight arcsecond threshold, rather than silently
          linking a non-physical jump; `gauss_preliminary_orbit()` is the
          classical (1809) angles-only, single-pass (3rd-order f/g series)
          method (e.g. Curtis's *Orbital Mechanics for Engineering
          Students*), taking each observation's real heliocentric observer
          position from astropy's builtin (no-network) solar-system
          ephemeris; `state_vector_to_elements()`/`elements_to_state_vector()`
          are the standard closed-form classical-element conversion;
          `two_body_propagate()` advances mean anomaly with no perturbations
          modelled. A synthetic main-belt-like test orbit (a=2.5 AU, e=0.15,
          i=10 deg) propagated to three epochs ~7 hours apart and observed
          from Earth's real ephemeris position is recovered by the full
          Gauss-method pipeline to better than 0.1% in semi-major axis and
          0.01 deg in inclination/node.
          Explicitly NOT attempted: iterative refinement of the preliminary
          orbit (universal-variable Kepler propagation feeding back into the
          f/g coefficients), light-time/aberration/topocentric-parallax
          correction, and planetary perturbations in propagation -- all
          standard next steps once a real multi-night, multi-survey tracklet
          exists to fit against; the single-pass method here is accurate
          enough for the closely-spaced (hours to about a day) observations
          a tracklet actually spans, which is what it is validated against.
          `orbital_element_residuals()` provides the "orbital-element
          residuals vs. MPC published elements" diagnostic this backlog item
          calls for; "tracklet-linking precision/recall" is exercised in
          `assemble_tracklets`'s own accept/reject test coverage, but only
          against synthetic detections -- a real precision/recall study
          needs the same real tracklet source this entry says does not exist
          yet. Like every other module in this family, NOT wired into
          `evidence.WEIGHTS`/`scoring.combine()`.
          Tests: `tests/test_moving_objects.py` (18 cases) -- orbit-recovery
          accuracy, degenerate-geometry error paths, tracklet accept/reject,
          and MPC response parsing/auth-header construction via a
          monkeypatched `netclient.get` (no live call).


---------------------------------------------------------------------
PHASE 4 — BASELINE ANOMALY DETECTION
---------------------------------------------------------------------

[PARTIAL] Image-derived features (plan section 12)
          A bounded FITS feature extractor now persists source checksum,
          background/MAD, thresholded morphology, centroid/moments,
          ellipticity, peak S/N, residual and target-centroid distance with a
          versioned schema. The candidate workspace can run it on a verified
          ZTF cutout. Versioned image sidecars are now persisted as keyed
          Parquet and can be explicitly left-joined to feature identities with
          match/missing diagnostics. PSF fitting, multi-source deblending, and
          publication-grade stellar photometry remain open.

[PARTIAL] Spectral features (plan section 12)
          A bounded FITS-table spectral extractor now validates wavelength,
          flux, errors/ivar, ordering, and point limits, then persists
          continuum, S/N, residual, line-threshold, and equivalent-width proxy
          features with provenance. SDSS metadata discovery is available as an
          opt-in connector. Versioned spectral sidecars now use the same keyed
          Parquet/join contract as image rows. Live spectrum retrieval,
          instrument-specific frame calibration, and publication-grade line
          modelling remain open.

[PARTIAL] Period search is single-band by default
          Lomb-Scargle still runs per light curve in the main matrix path, but
          multiband_periodic_features() now provides an opt-in shared-period
          fit for two or more bands. Full hierarchical modelling remains open.


[PARTIAL] Period search supports multi-band shared fitting
          A bounded joint Lomb-Scargle adapter now subtracts per-band baselines
          and fits a shared period across two or more bands. Full hierarchical
          heteroscedastic multi-band modelling remains open.
          UPDATE 2026-08-22: the hierarchical heteroscedastic piece is now
          built, as a separate, opt-in module rather than a change to the
          production `multiband.py` path: `multiband_hier.py` fits one
          shared latent period across bands via a celerite2 quasi-periodic
          Gaussian process (`terms.RotationTerm`) per band, all bands
          sharing `period` but each fitting its OWN amplitude (`sigma`) and
          its OWN jitter, with each band's real per-point measurement error
          feeding the GP noise model directly (heteroscedastic) rather than
          the single SNR-threshold screen `features.py` uses today.
          celerite2 is a genuinely new dependency, gated exactly like torch
          already is in this codebase: a new `research` extra in `engine
          pyproject.toml` (never in `requirements.lock`, the packaged-build
          install list), lazy-imported only inside the functions that need
          it, and stripped from the packaged CPU build by
          `scripts/build-engine.ps1` (its torch/cupy/dask exclusion list
          gained one more name).
          Explicitly a smaller, more constrained model than "full"
          hierarchical multiband GP fitting: the RotationTerm's overtone
          shape (Q0, dQ, f) is held FIXED and shared across bands and across
          the whole period grid -- only period and each band's own
          amplitude/jitter are fitted, a deliberate scope limit stated in
          the module's own docstring, not an oversight. Cost is O(grid_size
          * n_bands * n_points) per object (celerite2's banded solver, not a
          naive O(n^3) GP), so like `multiband.py` this stays an explicit
          per-object research action, never swept into
          `featurematrix.build()`.
          Validated against synthetic, known-truth multiband curves
          (recoverable by construction): a shared period is recovered
          within the grid's own resolution, per-band amplitude ORDERING is
          preserved (a brighter-amplitude band's fitted sigma exceeds a
          fainter one's), `credible_interval` returns a valid
          highest-posterior-density region from the log-likelihood profile,
          and `calibrated_fap`'s time-scrambled null-trial FAP is reliably
          low for a real injected period and reliably high for pure noise --
          this IS a validated MECHANISM, in the same sense the artifact-
          weight injection study above is: the code runs and produces
          directionally correct, reproducible results on synthetic data, not
          yet a large-scale, real-data statistical calibration. Like every
          other interpretation layer in this file, NOT wired into
          `evidence.WEIGHTS`/`scoring.combine()`.
          Tests: `tests/test_multiband_hier.py` (15 cases), skipped via
          `pytest.importorskip("celerite2")` when the optional `research`
          extra is not installed -- the same pattern `test_deep.py` already
          uses for torch.


[PARTIAL] Transit and TTV vetting (P1)  <-- NEW 2026-08-24
          `transit_ttv.py` (BLS search, limb-darkened model, model fit) and
          `transit_vetting.py` (per-transit timing/O-C, false-positive
          vetting) are a `moving_objects.py`-shaped standalone module pair,
          not a `SurveyConnector`: it operates on a light curve's real time
          series, already fetched via `tess_pixels.py`'s TESS TPF path, not
          a cone search. Split into two files purely to keep each under
          this project's 500-line guideline, mirroring the
          `stellar_manifold.py` / `stellar_manifold_eval.py` split, not two
          independent modules. `bls_search` is a thin, validated wrapper
          over `astropy.timeseries.BoxLeastSquares` (already a core
          dependency) using an explicit deterministic period grid rather
          than `autoperiod`'s frequency-oversampling optimisation -- a
          stated simplification, not a hidden one.
          `limb_darkened_transit_model` computes a circular-orbit, quadratic
          limb-darkened transit flux via analytic concentric-ring
          (annulus) circle-overlap integration -- each ring's blocked area
          is the closed-form two-circle lens-intersection formula, not a
          Cartesian point-grid count. A point-grid version was built first
          and found, this session, to be a genuine numerical dead end for
          model fitting: the blocked point SET only changes when a
          continuous parameter (t0/period/rp_rs/a_rs/inc_deg) moves far
          enough to cross a fixed grid-cell boundary, so
          `scipy.optimize.least_squares`'s finite-difference gradient
          measured as exactly zero for all five of those parameters --
          only u1/u2, which enter through a smooth formula rather than set
          membership, ever moved during a fit. The ring/analytic-overlap
          version is smooth in every parameter and was confirmed, this
          session, to actually converge t0/period/rp_rs/a_rs/inc_deg (not
          just u1/u2) on synthetic injected data: `rp_rs` recovered to
          within 0.1% and `period_days` to within 0.02% of the injected
          value. This is a real numerical bug found and fixed while
          building this module, worth recording the same way
          `multimodal_encoders.py`'s two numerical bugs are recorded above.
          Deliberately NOT the closed-form elliptic-integral solution of
          Mandel & Agol (2002, ApJ 580, L171) -- the ring integration is
          exact in the ring-count limit and avoids adding a compiled
          transit-modelling dependency (e.g. `batman`) purely to compute
          the same physical quantity `numpy`/`scipy` (both already core
          dependencies) can compute directly, the same "avoid a new
          dependency when the real answer is directly computable" choice
          the neural-ODE entry above made against `torchdiffeq`. Circular
          orbits only (e=0); primary transits only (no occultation/
          secondary-eclipse flux model -- `vet_candidate` searches for a
          secondary eclipse as a false-positive signature instead, which
          needs only a depth comparison, not its own flux model).
          `ttv_o_minus_c` is observed-minus-calculated (O-C) timing-residual
          analysis against a linear ephemeris from `per_transit_midpoints`'s
          per-transit local mid-time fits -- NOT a full N-body dynamical TTV
          integration (e.g. a specific resonant perturber's predicted
          signal). That remains open, the same "mechanism built, full
          multi-body physics deferred" scope statement
          `moving_objects.py`'s two-body-only `two_body_propagate` already
          carries. An epoch with too few in-window points is skipped, not
          degraded with a fabricated midpoint -- the same
          `tensors.resample` MIN_POINTS-skip convention used throughout
          this codebase; skipped epochs are returned separately.
          `vet_candidate`'s three checks (odd/even transit-depth mismatch,
          secondary-eclipse search, transit-shape flat-bottom-vs-V-shape
          fraction) are standard, citable heuristics from the
          transiting-planet vetting literature (e.g. the Kepler Robovetter,
          Thompson et al. 2018, ApJS 235, 38) -- bounded statistical/
          geometric checks, NOT pixel-level centroid vetting, which needs
          source-position-resolved photometry (`tess_psf.py`-level PSF
          fitting work) and is explicitly out of scope here.
          `exoplanet_archive.py` reuses `tap.py`'s existing bounded, cached,
          read-only TAP/ADQL client (unmodified) against the NASA Exoplanet
          Archive's `ps` table for a diagnostic period/depth cross-check
          against a published confirmed-planet record -- never a correction
          applied to a fitted result and never folded into ranking, the
          same "catalog_relative, not resolved-confirmation" restraint
          `blend.source_attribution` already applies. `netclient.py` gained
          an `"exoplanetarchive"` rate-limit bucket (1.0s, the same
          TAP-sync-is-a-database-job rationale as `datalab`). A real bug
          was found and fixed via a live check this session: the endpoint
          is `https://exoplanetarchive.ipac.caltech.edu/TAP/sync`, not the
          bare `/TAP` path an earlier version used, which 404s -- confirmed
          by a direct `curl` request and then by
          `tests/test_exoplanet_archive_live.py` (live-marked, skipped by
          default, run via `pytest tests/test_exoplanet_archive_live.py -m
          live`), which queries Kepler-10 and asserts the documented
          `_PS_COLUMNS` field names (`pl_name`, `hostname`, `pl_orbper`,
          `pl_trandur`, `pl_trandep`, ...) parse a real returned row with a
          plausible orbital period. This is one live-endpoint contract
          confirmed, not exhaustively validated across the whole `ps`
          schema -- the same partial-validation caveat every connector in
          this file carries.
          Kepler/K2 light-curve acquisition (the third data source this
          backlog item names) and a genuine multi-survey-scale recall/
          false-positive-rate/TTV-error study (the item's own named
          metrics, beyond the per-function synthetic-recovery checks above)
          remain open -- `lightkurve` (already a core dependency) has a
          built-in MAST search that could supply Kepler/K2 light curves
          without new HTTP code, but wiring that adapter and running the
          injection-recovery study it would enable at real scale is
          explicitly NOT done this session, the same "mechanism validated
          on synthetic ground truth, not yet run at real Stage-B scale"
          caveat `open_world_injection.py`/`stellar_manifold_eval.py`
          already carry for their own studies.
          Like every other opt-in research module in this file, NOT wired
          into `rpc.py`, `scoring.WEIGHTS`, or `evidence.py`.
          Tests: `tests/test_transit_ttv.py` (31 cases: BLS recovery,
          limb-darkening closed-form limits, model parameter validation,
          fit convergence and its failure modes, duration-formula
          correctness, per-transit midpoint recovery and sparse-epoch
          skipping, O-C residual arithmetic, and each vetting heuristic's
          accept/reject behavior on constructed synthetic light curves, plus
          a static `rpc.py`-non-reference check) and
          `tests/test_exoplanet_archive.py` (6 cases: ADQL construction,
          quote escaping, response parsing, service-failure propagation,
          and the published-value comparison diagnostic), and
          `tests/test_exoplanet_archive_live.py` (1 case, live-marked).


[PARTIAL] Eclipsing-binary physical fitting (P1)  <-- NEW 2026-08-24
          `eclipsing_binary.py` (geometry model + fit) and
          `eclipsing_binary_dimensions.py` (absolute radii/masses, split
          purely for the 500-line guideline, same
          `stellar_manifold.py`/`stellar_manifold_eval.py` rationale) are a
          `transit_ttv.py`-shaped module pair for a genuinely new case:
          TWO finite, mutually eclipsing, self-luminous bodies rather than
          one dark planet transiting a much bigger star. `transit_ttv.py`'s
          public `limb_darkened_transit_model` could not be reused directly
          -- its own `rp_rs <= 1` validation assumes the occulter is always
          smaller than the occulted star, false in general for two
          comparably-sized stars -- so `eclipsing_binary_model` instead
          calls the private `_limb_darkening_rings`/`_disk_overlap_area`
          ring/lens-area helpers directly (reused, not reimplemented) and
          adds its own primary/secondary bookkeeping (which body is in
          front alternates every half period) and Stefan-Boltzmann
          luminosity weighting (`L ~ R^2 * Teff^4` via a caller-supplied
          `teff_ratio`) between the two bodies' contributions. Parameterised
          by `r1_a`/`r2_a` (each body's radius in units of the orbital
          semi-major axis, the standard EB-literature convention) rather
          than `transit_ttv.py`'s "one radius in units of the other"
          convention, precisely so an occulter larger than the occulted
          body is never an invalid input. Verified this session: primary
          deeper than secondary for a cooler second body; equal depths for
          equal radius/temperature; a larger occulter's mid-eclipse flux
          matches the exact closed-form luminosity ratio
          r2_a^2/(r1_a^2+r2_a^2) to within grid-resolution precision; and
          `fit_eclipsing_binary`'s `scipy.optimize.least_squares` refinement
          recovers all ten injected synthetic parameters (t0, period,
          r1_a, r2_a, inc, teff_ratio to within ~1%; u1/u2 to the same
          known degeneracy `transit_ttv.py`'s own fit already carries).
          Circular orbits only (e=0, inherited from `transit_ttv.py`); no
          reflection effect or ellipsoidal (tidal-distortion) variation --
          both real light-curve-shaping physics for close binaries,
          explicitly not modelled, stated up front rather than glossed
          over. `primary_secondary_depths`/`temperature_ratio_from_depths`
          use the standard eclipse-depth Teff-ratio approximation
          (`(secondary_depth/primary_depth)**0.25`), a real simplification
          (assumes comparable limb darkening, negligible reflection), not a
          full spectral-synthesis solution.

          The "RV-inspired" half: this codebase has no real radial-velocity
          data source (grep confirmed, greenfield). Real absolute-dimension
          EB work normally uses RV semi-amplitudes to get the physical
          semi-major axis directly; `eclipsing_binary_dimensions.py`
          substitutes a real technique used in the literature when RV is
          unavailable instead of fabricating one: anchor ONE body's
          physical radius from Gaia photometry
          (`astrophysical_parameters.radius_gspphot`, or a CMD-Teff
          fallback via the already-existing
          `stellar_manifold.nearest_track_point`), propagate that anchor
          through the light-curve-fitted radius RATIO to get both bodies'
          physical radii and the physical semi-major axis, apply Kepler's
          third law for the TOTAL system mass, then split it between the
          bodies using a real mass-Teff relation (`MASS_RADIUS_ZAMS_TRACK`)
          evaluated at each body's own anchor-derived temperature, not an
          assumed equal split. `MASS_RADIUS_ZAMS_TRACK` extends
          `stellar_manifold.ZAMS_TRACK`'s own real source (Eric Mamajek's
          maintained dwarf-star table, version 2022.04.16) with that same
          table's `R_Rsun`/`Msun` columns for the identical B9V-M7V rows --
          live-fetched this session from
          https://www.pas.rochester.edu/~emamajek/EEM_dwarf_UBVIJHK_colors_Teff.txt
          (the plain `http://` URL `stellar_manifold.py`'s own docstring
          cites now 302-redirects to this `https://` one; same host,
          confirmed by direct download and column extraction, not assumed
          unchanged from that entry's 2026-08-22 fetch). Verified end-to-end
          against a self-consistent synthetic
          binary (real G1V/K1V table rows as ground truth): recovered
          r1/r2/a/total_mass/m1/m2 all match to better than 1e-6 relative
          error, the arithmetic itself being exact once the anchor is
          supplied -- the real uncertainty in this technique is entirely in
          how good the anchor and the ZAMS mass-Teff assumption are for a
          REAL, possibly evolved or interacting star, which the ONE static
          ZAMS-track scope limit (stated in `stellar_manifold.py` and
          inherited here) already names.

          `surveys/gaia.py` gained `query_eclipsing_binary`/
          `query_astrophysical_parameters`, both via the
          `astroquery.gaia.Gaia.launch_job` client this file's `cone_search`
          already uses. Two real column-shape corrections were found and
          fixed via live checks this session, not assumed from
          documentation: `gaiadr3.vari_eclipsing_binary` has NO `period`
          column at all -- confirmed live via `tap_schema.columns` -- only
          `frequency` (unit `1/d`, so `period_days = 1.0 / frequency`) and
          `derived_primary_ecl_phase/duration/depth`
          (`derived_secondary_*` likewise); an initial guess at
          `ecl_dur_prim`/`ecl_dur_sec`/`period` column names 400'd against
          the real service. `gaiadr3.astrophysical_parameters`'s
          `radius_gspphot`/`teff_gspphot`/`mass_flame`/`radius_flame`
          column names were confirmed correct on the first live query.
          Both functions were then run against a real Gaia DR3 source
          (5277210824652602624) and returned real, physically plausible
          values (period ~0.568 d, primary depth ~0.44; radius_gspphot
          ~1.06 Rsun; mass_flame/radius_flame absent for this particular
          source, returned as `None` rather than fabricated).

          `query_component_catalog`/`mass_radius_residuals` provide the
          "Metrics: Mass/radius residuals against EB catalogs" cross-check
          this backlog item names, reusing `catalogs.py`'s `_fetch_vsx`-
          shaped VizieR query pattern against VizieR `J/ApJ/709/535` (Brown
          2010, "Eclipsing binary stars with accurate mass and radius
          estimates") -- identified this session via `Vizier.find_catalogs`
          (not assumed from memory) and confirmed live by cone-searching
          real eclipsing binary V760 Sco's real published position and
          getting back its real component-B row (Mass=4.609 Msun,
          Rad=2.642 Rsun, Teff=16300 K). Diagnostic only, reported
          residuals never correct a fitted result, the same
          `blend.source_attribution`/`exoplanet_archive.compare_to_published`
          restraint every prior cross-check in this codebase applies.

          Like every other opt-in research module in this file, NOT wired
          into `rpc.py`, `scoring.WEIGHTS`, or `evidence.py`.
          Tests: `tests/test_eclipsing_binary.py` (18 cases: baseline/
          primary-secondary depth relationships, the closed-form total-
          eclipse luminosity-ratio check, parameter validation, fit
          convergence and its failure modes, depth/temperature-ratio
          arithmetic, and a static `rpc.py`-non-reference check),
          `tests/test_eclipsing_binary_dimensions.py` (12 cases: mass-Teff
          table lookup and clamping, radius-anchor preference/fallback/
          failure, the self-consistent synthetic-binary recovery, and the
          catalog cross-check's parsing/residual arithmetic),
          `tests/test_surveys_gaia.py` (5 cases: the two new Gaia query
          functions' real-column-shape parsing, empty-result handling, and
          input validation), and
          `tests/test_eclipsing_binary_dimensions_live.py` (3 cases,
          live-marked).


[PARTIAL] Stellar-flare energy inference (P1)  <-- NEW 2026-08-24
          `flare.py` (template, detection, fit, equivalent duration) and
          `flare_energy.py` (bolometric energy + synthetic
          injection-recovery study, split purely for the 500-line
          guideline, same `stellar_manifold.py`/`stellar_manifold_eval.py`
          rationale) are `transit_ttv.py`-shaped modules for a genuinely
          new physical shape: no flare template existed anywhere in this
          codebase before this session. `evaluate.py`'s synthetic
          `"flare"` injection kind (one of `ANOMALY_KINDS`) was checked and
          confirmed inadequate for energy modelling -- an instant-rise,
          pure-exponential-decay shape with no polynomial rise phase and no
          two-component decay, used only as a generic anomaly-injection
          sanity check (`open_world_injection.py`'s own docstring already
          calls this out as one of "4 hand-coded shapes") -- and is NOT
          reused here. `davenport_flare_template` instead implements the
          real, published analytic template (Davenport et al. 2014, ApJ
          797, 122): a piecewise 4th-order-polynomial rise plus a
          two-exponential decay, the same template widely reproduced in
          flare-fitting tools (e.g. appaloosa/altaipony's `aflare`). The
          template carries a real, small (~0.8%) discontinuity at t'=0
          between its own published rise (1.0 exactly) and decay
          (0.6890+0.3030=0.992) pieces -- an artefact of the original
          published fit, not a bug introduced here.

          `equivalent_duration` (ED; Gershberg 1972, standard in Hawley et
          al. 2014/Davenport 2016) is a real, exact trapezoidal
          time-integral of relative flux excess, needing no assumed
          physics -- verified against a known closed-form triangular-pulse
          integral. `relative_flux_excess` converts either a raw TESS
          `flux` (electrons/sec, no zero-point) or ZTF `mag` light curve
          into a common relative-excess representation
          (`flux/quiescent_flux - 1`, or the magnitude equivalent) via a
          local rolling-median baseline, needing NO absolute flux
          calibration for either survey -- confirmed neither
          `surveys/tess.py` nor `surveys/ztf.py` publishes one, and ED only
          ever needs a RATIO. Verified this session that the flux-kind and
          mag-kind paths agree to floating-point precision on the same
          injected signal. `detect_flare_candidates` is a bounded "N
          consecutive points above a sigma threshold" heuristic (e.g.
          Chang et al. 2015) -- detection only, no false-positive vetting
          beyond the run-length requirement, a real stated scope limit.
          `fit_flare_template`'s `scipy.optimize.least_squares` refinement
          recovers injected `t_peak`/`fwhm`/`amplitude` to a few percent on
          synthetic data; single-peak flares only, no complex/multi-peak
          morphology, a real stated scope limit.

          `flare_energy.py` adds this codebase's first ABSOLUTE (not
          ratio-only) stellar luminosity computation --
          `eclipsing_binary.py`'s own Stefan-Boltzmann weighting only ever
          needed a RATIO between two bodies, never an erg/s value, so no
          prior module needed real physical constants for it.
          `quiescent_bolometric_luminosity`'s `sigma_SB`/`R_sun` constants
          were verified this session against the real nominal solar
          luminosity, not assumed correct: `quiescent_bolometric_luminosity
          (radius_solar=1.0, teff_k=5772.0)` returns 3.828e33 erg/s to 4
          significant figures, matching the real IAU nominal solar
          luminosity constant. `bolometric_flare_energy`'s `E = ED * L`
          relation is the standard Hawley et al. (2014)/Davenport (2016)
          PASSBAND-flux-excess approximation to bolometric energy --
          explicitly NOT the more sophisticated two-blackbody
          bandpass-correction method (comparing a ~9000-10000 K flare
          blackbody against the star's own cooler photosphere, integrated
          against the real TESS/Kepler response function), which needs a
          real instrument response curve this codebase does not have and
          would be a second, separable acquisition task -- stated as
          explicitly open, not glossed over, the same restraint
          `transit_ttv.py` states for its own "no occultation dimming"
          limit. Radius/Teff anchoring is left to the caller via
          `eclipsing_binary_dimensions.anchor_physical_radius`/
          `mass_and_radius_at_teff` (item 17's real Gaia-photometry-or-
          ZAMS-fallback anchor, reused unchanged, not reimplemented).

          `evaluate_flare_recovery` answers this backlog item's own named
          metrics (energy bias, flare completeness, duration/amplitude
          calibration) on SYNTHETIC ground truth (a flat baseline plus
          Gaussian noise, not a real stored light curve) -- the same
          "mechanism validated on synthetic data, not yet run at real
          Stage-B scale" caveat every other eval module in this family
          (`stellar_manifold_eval.py`, `multiband_hier.py`) already
          carries; running this against real TESS/ZTF baselines remains
          open. Verified this session on a small amplitude grid:
          completeness rises from 50% at the faintest injected amplitude to
          100% by the second grid point, and energy fractional bias shrinks
          from roughly -12% to -4% as amplitude increases -- both
          directionally correct, reproducible behaviour, not yet a
          large-scale calibration.

          Kepler acquisition remains the SAME open gap item 16's own
          `docs/DEFERRED.txt` entry already named (a `lightkurve`-based
          Kepler/K2 fetch was scoped as explicitly open there) -- restated
          here as still open, not re-solved, since this module consumes
          whatever light curve it is given rather than acquiring one
          itself.

          Like every other opt-in research module in this file, NOT wired
          into `rpc.py`, `scoring.WEIGHTS`, or `evidence.py`.
          Tests: `tests/test_flare.py` (16 cases: template shape/
          continuity, relative-flux-excess conversion for both `flux` and
          `mag` `value_kind` cross-checked against each other, detection
          recall/no-false-positive behaviour, fit convergence and its
          failure modes, ED arithmetic against a closed-form triangular
          integral, and a static `rpc.py`-non-reference check) and
          `tests/test_flare_energy.py` (11 cases: the Stefan-Boltzmann
          solar-luminosity consistency check, `R^2*Teff^4` scaling,
          `E=ED*L` arithmetic, and the injection-recovery study's
          completeness/energy-bias behaviour).


[PARTIAL] Early supernova classification plus photo-z (P1)  <-- NEW 2026-08-24
          `sn_classification.py` (Bazin model, point-estimate + Bayesian
          MCMC fit, truncation, features) and `sn_classification_eval.py`
          (time-to-classification study, split purely for the 500-line
          guideline) cover the first thread; `photo_z.py` covers the
          second. Both were genuinely greenfield -- no photo-z, redshift,
          Bazin/SALT2/sncosmo, or NMAD code existed anywhere in this
          codebase before this session (grepped, zero hits).

          `bazin_model` is the standard parametric SN rise/decay
          light-curve model (Bazin et al. 2009, A&A 499, 653):
          `f(t) = A*exp(-(t-t0)/tau_fall)/(1+exp(-(t-t0)/tau_rise)) + B`.
          `fit_bazin_posterior` is this module's own `emcee`-based MCMC
          wrapper (the citable "Bayesian evolving light-curve model" this
          backlog item names), following `microlensing_fit.
          sample_posterior`'s exact pattern (flat prior in bounds, Gaussian
          `-chi2/2` likelihood, walker jitter around a point estimate,
          autocorrelation-based `converged` reporting, the same
          `emcee.autocorr` logger-silencing) -- that function itself is
          hardcoded to microlensing's own three-parameter model and could
          not be imported directly. One real, minor type-purity bug was
          fixed while copying that pattern: the original's
          `converged = bool(finite_tau) and n_steps >= 50*max(finite_tau)`
          leaves `converged` as `np.bool_`, not a Python `bool`, since the
          right operand of `and` is returned as-is when the left is
          truthy -- caught by this module's own `isinstance(converged,
          bool)` test, fixed here with an explicit `bool(...)` wrap.
          `microlensing_fit.py` itself carries the same latent expression,
          out of scope to touch for this item, noted here for the record.
          Running full MCMC per truncated curve per object per cutoff day
          across a study would be prohibitively slow, so the bulk
          early-classification study instead uses
          `fit_bazin_point_estimate` (fast `scipy.optimize.least_squares`,
          the same pattern every other `fit_*` in this codebase family
          uses) for per-cutoff feature extraction, reserving the full MCMC
          posterior for one object at a time -- a real, stated
          speed/rigor split, not a hidden shortcut.

          Neither ALeRCE (`surveys/alerce.py`'s `query_classified_objects`/
          `fetch_light_curves`, already real and live, reused unchanged)
          nor this codebase has a server-side "as-of-date" query --
          `truncate_light_curve` IS the entire "early alert" simulation:
          an already-fully-fetched real light curve is truncated
          client-side to what would have been visible N days after first
          detection. `evaluate_time_to_classification`'s labels are real
          ALeRCE broker classifications taken as ground truth for this
          downstream evaluation, the same precedent `open_world_
          injection.py` already set for using ALeRCE's real classifications
          as a held-out truth set. Its classifier follows `multimodal_eval.
          linear_probe_macro_f1`'s exact `LogisticRegression` ->
          `f1_score(average="macro")` pattern -- that function is coupled
          to this codebase's torch multimodal embeddings and not directly
          importable, so this module reimplements the same shape over
          plain feature arrays. "Time-to-classification" has no prior
          definition in this codebase or, to this module's knowledge, one
          single universally agreed literature definition; the one used
          here (first cutoff day where mean macro-F1 reaches and stays at
          or above 80% of the asymptotic, full-light-curve macro-F1) is
          stated explicitly as this module's own convention, verified on a
          synthetic two-class (fast-declining vs. slow-declining Bazin
          shape) set this session: macro-F1 rose from a 3-day-cutoff mean
          of 0.84 to 1.0 at full light curve, with `time_to_classification
          =15` days under that definition -- a real, reproducible,
          directionally-correct result on synthetic data, not yet run
          against a real multi-class ALeRCE-labelled sample at scale.
          `bazin_features` always returns a fixed-length vector (real
          non-parametric shape statistics plus an explicit
          `fit_converged` flag) so a too-sparse early truncation degrades
          gracefully rather than crashing the downstream classifier or
          fabricating fit parameters. TNS remains credential-gated,
          confirmed still unavailable by default; not used here, the same
          restatement item 18 already made for Kepler acquisition.

          `photo_z.py` is empirical (training-set) photo-z --
          k-nearest-neighbour regression in photometry space (e.g. Csabai
          et al. 2003) via `sklearn.neighbors.KNeighborsRegressor`
          (`scikit-learn` already a core dependency) -- explicitly NOT SED
          template fitting, which would need a real galaxy/QSO spectral
          template library this codebase does not have. `photo_z_nmad`
          implements the standard `1.4826*median(|z_pred-z_true|/
          (1+z_true))` definition (`alerce.py`'s own `1.4826` MAD-scaling
          constant was precedent for the factor, not this formula).
          `build_calibration_sample` reuses `crossmatch.match_catalogs`
          unchanged (crossmatch.py:211) to cross-match real DES/Pan-STARRS
          multi-band photometry (both connectors' `cone_search` already
          returns real magnitudes in `SourceRef.extra`, no new connector
          code needed) against a REAL spectroscopic redshift sample.

          `surveys/sdss.py` gained `query_spectroscopic_redshifts`
          (`SpecObjAll.z`/`zErr`, a new additive function, `cone_search`
          unchanged) -- and, while live-verifying it, uncovered and fixed
          a genuine, pre-existing bug in `parse_csv` that predates this
          session and had no live test to catch it: the real SkyServer
          SqlSearch endpoint prefixes its CSV response with a `#Table1`
          comment line before the true header row (confirmed live,
          2026-08-24), which `csv.DictReader` was silently misparsing as
          the header itself, so EVERY live query through this file --
          `cone_search` included, not just the new function -- returned
          zero rows despite the raw HTTP response containing real data. A
          live cone search that worked correctly via `curl` returned zero
          `SourceRef`s through this codebase before the fix, and returned
          the same 5 real rows after it, confirmed directly. `parse_csv`
          now strips any leading `#`-prefixed line before parsing;
          `tests/test_surveys_sdss.py` gained both an offline regression
          test for the stripping behaviour and a new `live`-marked test
          class guarding against this exact failure mode recurring.

          Like every other opt-in research module in this file, NOT wired
          into `rpc.py`, `scoring.WEIGHTS`, or `evidence.py`.
          Tests: `tests/test_sn_classification.py` (17 cases: Bazin model
          shape/baseline-recovery/parameter-validation, point-estimate and
          MCMC fit recovery on synthetic injected parameters, truncation
          correctness, feature-extraction fit-vs-fallback behaviour, and
          the time-to-classification study's metric arithmetic on a
          synthetic two-class set, plus a static `rpc.py`-non-reference
          check), `tests/test_photo_z.py` (12 cases: NMAD arithmetic
          against a hand-computed value and against a perfect prediction,
          kNN recovery of a known deterministic colour-redshift relation,
          and the calibration-sample cross-match's pairing/skip/
          validation behaviour), and `tests/test_surveys_sdss.py`
          (extended to 25 cases: the `#Table1` parse fix, the new
          redshift-query function's parsing/fallback/validation, and one
          `live`-marked case).


[PARTIAL] TDE/AGN change-point and reverberation analysis (P1)  <-- NEW 2026-08-24
          The last item on the external roadmap table this multi-session
          plan has been working through (items 16-20; all five now
          `[DONE]`, this entry included). `agn_changepoint.py` (DRW model,
          TDE flare model, change-point evidence) and `agn_changepoint_
          eval.py` (lead-time study, split purely for the 500-line
          guideline) cover the first thread; `reverberation.py` covers the
          second. Both genuinely greenfield -- no change-point/
          reverberation/DRW/CCF/CARMA/TDE code existed anywhere in this
          codebase before this session (grepped, zero relevant hits; the
          existing production `features.change_point_score` is an
          unrelated simple rolling-window statistic, its own docstring
          already says "not a formal change-point model," not touched).

          `fit_drw` reuses `multiband_hier.py`'s exact `celerite2`
          invocation pattern (`_require_celerite2()` lazy-import guard,
          `GaussianProcess(term, mean=...)` -> `gp.compute` ->
          `gp.log_likelihood`), swapping `terms.RotationTerm`
          (quasi-periodic) for `terms.RealTerm(a, c)` -- celerite2's real,
          documented pure-exponential kernel `a*exp(-c*|dt|)`, exactly the
          standard AGN damped-random-walk covariance (Kelly et al. 2009,
          ApJ 698, 895; MacLeod et al. 2010, ApJ 721, 1014). Verified this
          session: DRW `(sigma, tau)` recovery is loose on a baseline only
          ~16x `tau` (a real, documented DRW-estimation property, not a
          bug) and tight on a baseline ~170x `tau` (sigma to 6%, tau to
          13%) -- confirming the OPTIMIZATION itself is correct, with the
          baseline-length sensitivity being genuine astrophysics.

          `celerite2.GaussianProcess(kernel, mean=...)` accepting a plain
          Python callable was confirmed by direct API inspection this
          session (`celerite2==0.3.3`) rather than assumed -- this is what
          makes `changepoint_evidence` one consistent GP log-likelihood
          comparison (DRW-only mean vs. DRW+flare mean) instead of a
          separate GP-whitened-residual step. `tde_flare_model` is a
          continuous Gaussian-rise/`t^-5/3`-power-law-decay empirical
          parametrization (`5/3` is the standard TDE fallback-accretion-
          rate power law, Rees 1988, Nature 333, 523), explicitly NOT a
          relativistic disruption simulation.

          A real optimizer-initialization bug was found and fixed this
          session: `changepoint_evidence`'s flare-parameter search is a
          local optimizer (`L-BFGS-B`); an initial `t0` guess seeded at a
          fixed, uninformative time (first tried: the end of the light
          curve) converged to a spurious near-zero-amplitude "no flare"
          solution even when a real, large, unambiguous flare was present
          in the data -- confirmed directly this session (delta_bic
          +21 instead of the correct -14 on the same injected flare).
          Fixed with `default_flare_guess`, which seeds `t0` at the
          largest excursion from the DRW mean; the same injected flare
          then correctly converges (delta_bic -14, fitted parameters
          within a few percent of the true injected values). This is
          exactly the kind of thing this codebase's own "verified, not
          assumed" discipline exists to catch.

          `calibrate_changepoint_significance` draws synthetic DRW-only
          realizations at the fitted `(sigma, tau)` via `celerite2`'s own
          `gp.sample()` (confirmed this session: `sample()` has no
          explicit-random-generator keyword, drawing from NumPy's global
          random state instead -- reproducibility here means seeding that
          global state locally, a real, discovered library constraint,
          not a design choice) and reports the delta-BIC value at a target
          false-positive rate -- the "class calibration" metric this
          backlog item names, a real null-population calibration in the
          same spirit as `association.calibrate_event_graph`'s scrambled-
          time-slide null (not directly reusable: that function operates
          on discrete event lists, this on one continuous light curve).

          `evaluate_lead_time` reuses `sn_classification.
          truncate_light_curve` UNCHANGED for the cutoff-grid early-alert
          simulation -- the identical mechanism item 19 built, now applied
          to flagging a change-point instead of a class label. "Lead
          time" is defined explicitly (no prior art in this codebase or a
          single agreed literature definition): true flare peak time MINUS
          the earliest cutoff at which the change-point is correctly
          flagged and remains flagged at every later grid point -- the
          same "does not drop below at any later point" discipline item
          19's time-to-classification definition uses. Verified this
          session on a clear synthetic injection (DRW sigma=0.2, tau=25,
          flare amplitude=1.5): 5/5 trials detected, consistent lead time
          across trials (mechanism validated; the specific grid used was
          coarse, so the exact lead-time value reported there is a
          reproducibility check, not a calibrated real-world number).

          `reverberation.py` is the second thread: `interpolated_cross_
          correlation` (Gaskell & Peterson 1987, ApJ 314, 738, the
          standard ICCF) and `lag_uncertainty_frss` (the standard Flux
          Randomization/Random Subset Selection Monte Carlo, Peterson et
          al. 1998, PASP 110, 660; 2004, ApJ 613, 682) needed no new
          dependency (bounded `numpy`/`scipy` interpolation/correlation
          arithmetic). A second real bug was found and fixed this session:
          the initial centroid-lag implementation averaged over every lag
          ANYWHERE in the grid clearing `0.8*peak correlation`, which for
          a periodic/quasi-periodic driving signal (the synthetic
          validation curve, and realistically many real AGN light curves
          with quasi-periodic structure) picks up unrelated alias lobes
          far from the true peak -- confirmed directly: a noiseless
          closed-form 12-day-shifted synthetic pair recovered a peak_lag
          of exactly 12.0 (correct) but a centroid_lag of -5.88 (wrong).
          Fixed to restrict the centroid to the CONTIGUOUS run of grid
          points around the peak that stays above threshold (the standard
          ICCF convention, e.g. the PyCCF implementation) -- the same
          closed-form case then recovers centroid_lag=12.0006. A related,
          earlier bug (an `or np.nan` shortcut that would have silently
          conflated a legitimate zero correlation with "no overlapping
          data," since `0.0` is falsy in Python) was caught and fixed
          before it shipped, during this same implementation pass.
          `evaluate_lag_recovery` generates its synthetic driving curve
          via `celerite2` (a real DRW realization, not a periodic toy
          signal, matching real AGN continuum variability) convolved with
          a known Gaussian transfer function -- **lag recovery**, the
          third named metric, verified this session: a 15-day true lag
          recovered as 15.22 +/- 0.27 (mean/std across 5 trials) on a
          representative synthetic case.

          No NEOWISE connector exists in this codebase at all (grepped,
          zero real hits) -- both new modules are written survey-agnostic
          (operate on any `time`/`value`/`value_err` arrays) specifically
          so they are ready to consume NEOWISE W1/W2 whenever that
          connector exists; this is a genuine, stated-as-open acquisition
          gap, the same restatement convention items 18-19 used for
          Kepler/TNS, not attempted here. `surveys/swift.py`/`xmm.py`/
          `chandra.py` remain explicitly metadata-only (SXPS/4XMM-DR13, no
          light curves, stated in their own docstrings) -- unrelated to
          this gap, not a Swift/XMM shortfall.

          Like every other opt-in research module in this file, NOT wired
          into `rpc.py`, `scoring.WEIGHTS`, or `evidence.py`.
          Tests: `tests/test_agn_changepoint.py` (12 cases: DRW recovery,
          flare-model shape/validation, change-point evidence on pure
          noise vs. a real injection, the `default_flare_guess` fix,
          significance-calibration behaviour, and a static `rpc.py`-non-
          reference check), `tests/test_agn_changepoint_eval.py` (6 cases:
          lead-time detection on a clear injection, zero-detection on a
          negligible one, and input validation), and `tests/test_
          reverberation.py` (11 cases: closed-form lag recovery noiseless
          and with noise, the zero-correlation-is-not-missing-data
          regression case, FR/RSS coverage of the true lag, and the
          synthetic lag-recovery study's bias/precision behaviour). All
          three celerite2-touching test files are gated with
          `pytest.importorskip("celerite2", ...)`, the same pattern
          `test_multiband_hier.py` already uses.


[PARTIAL] Live calibrated spectroscopy (P0)  <-- NEW 2026-08-24
          First item on the NEXT external roadmap table batch this
          multi-session plan is working through (items 21-25). Item 24 was
          taken first despite being numbered last in that batch because it
          is the batch's only P0, and item 25 (Bayesian spectral
          line-profile inference) depends on its spectrum-acquisition
          machinery.

          `spectroscopy_calibration.py` (wavelength-calibration and
          instrument-response diagnostics) and `spectroscopy_calibration_
          eval.py` (redshift/line residual validation, split purely for the
          500-line guideline) are genuinely greenfield -- no line-list
          cross-correlation or continuum-smoothness code existed anywhere in
          this codebase before this session. `independent_redshift_from_
          lines` grid-searches redshift by matching continuum-subtracted S/N
          peaks/troughs against a standard rest-frame line list (the same
          lines the SDSS pipeline itself targets); `continuum_smoothness_
          residual` is a bounded low-order-polynomial-residual proxy for
          instrument-response quality. `spectroscopy_calibration_eval.
          redshift_residuals` reuses `microlensing_eval.parameter_bias`
          UNCHANGED (its `{name: value}` row shape generalises to a
          redshift exactly as it does to a microlensing parameter) rather
          than reimplementing median-fractional-bias/MAD-scatter arithmetic
          a third time in this codebase.

          Three real bugs were found and fixed this session via synthetic
          recovery checks, all in `_continuum`/`find_candidate_lines`:
          (1) `np.convolve(..., mode="same")`'s implicit zero-padding
          collapsed the continuum estimate toward zero within half a
          window of either spectral edge, producing spurious S/N > 40
          "line" detections at both edges of an otherwise pure-noise
          spectrum -- fixed by excluding that boundary margin from peak
          detection. (2) A plain moving-average continuum leaks a strong,
          narrow line's own flux into the estimate at nearby wavelengths,
          understating it there -- confirmed directly, an isolated,
          cleanly-detected 56-sigma emission line produced a RING of ten
          spurious ~5-sigma "absorption" detections flanking it, each one
          just this continuum-estimation artifact, not a real feature --
          fixed by switching `_continuum` to a rolling MEDIAN filter
          (`scipy.ndimage.median_filter`, robust to a narrow feature within
          the window, unlike a mean). (3) `independent_redshift_from_lines`'
          `rest_lines or DEFAULT_REST_LINES` fallback silently replaced an
          explicitly passed empty dict with the default line list (an empty
          dict is falsy in Python) -- fixed to check for `None` specifically.
          A fourth issue was a real methodological limitation rather than a
          bug: a fixed-point-count redshift grid can be coarser than the
          velocity-tolerance window being searched for at a wide `z_max`,
          missing a true redshift that a genuinely matching line was
          detected for -- fixed by tying grid spacing directly to
          `velocity_tolerance_kms` (5x oversampled) instead of a fixed
          point count.

          A GENUINE, STATED (not silently worked around) limitation:
          `independent_redshift_from_lines` cannot disambiguate a spectrum
          with only ONE significant line -- H-alpha at z=0.02 and
          Lyman-alpha at z=4.5 place a line at the identical observed
          wavelength, so several very different redshifts can score
          equally well with no physical prior to break the tie. This is the
          same reason real spectroscopic pipelines require multiple line
          coincidences before trusting a redshift, confirmed directly this
          session (a single-line synthetic test was unrecoverable; the
          identical setup with 2-3 real lines recovered correctly, both
          checked and both now encoded in the test suite). Stated in the
          module's own docstring, not fixed by adding physical priors
          (expected line ratios, a template) outside this module's stated
          bounded scope.

          SDSS-V needed NO new connector, confirmed live this session
          rather than assumed: `sdss.py`'s existing `SDSSConnector` and
          `query_spectroscopic_redshifts` work completely unchanged with
          `release="dr19"` (SDSS-V's current DR) -- same SQL endpoint, same
          schema, same `#Table1` CSV quirk, real rows returned. `SpecObjAll`
          on DR19 was also confirmed this session to carry ONLY
          legacy-generation (pre-FPS) reductions -- a live query for
          `run2d='v6_1_3'` (SDSS-V's own new fiber-positioner-era reduction,
          confirmed to exist as a real directory on the SAS) returned zero
          rows, so "SDSS-V support" today means legacy SDSS spectra visible
          through DR19's catalog, not new FPS-era observations. The new
          FPS-era spectrum files were confirmed to exist on disk under a
          materially different naming convention (a long CatalogID or
          coordinate string in the fiber-number slot, not a small
          zero-padded integer) but are unreachable from this connector since
          `SpecObjAll` carries none of those rows -- stated as a real, open
          [GAP], not guessed at. `fetch_spectrum`'s existing dr17-hosted
          `SPECTRUM_URL` was independently confirmed correct for a real
          DR19-cataloged legacy row too: SDSS spectrum files live at one
          stable, DR-independent SAS location once published, which is also
          why this module never needed a release-dependent URL.

DESI's catalog/redshift lookup is now FIXED (see the item-24 history above for
the original [BLOCKED] finding). Two follow-up sessions on 2026-08-25:

Session 1 tried the SPARCL pivot `tap.py`'s original entry named as "the more
direct long-term fix", and REVERTED it -- not viable for this codebase as it
stands. `pip install sparclclient` (v1.3.0) force-downgrades `numpy` to
`1.26.4` via its `specutils`/`asdf-astropy`/`gwcs` dependency chain, a hard
conflict with this project's pinned `numpy==2.5.2`. `pip`'s resolver flagged
the conflict but installed anyway (no hard failure), so the breakage was
SILENT until something needing the newer numpy was actually imported --
confirmed live: `astropy_healpix` (`gw.py`/`kilonova_eval.py`'s own
dependency) failed outright with a binary C-API mismatch (`numpy._core.
multiarray failed to import`) after the install, and `photutils`/`cupy`
reported the identical incompatibility. Fully reverted (`sparclclient` and
its forced dependency chain uninstalled; `numpy==2.5.2`/`scipy==1.18.0`/
`astropy==8.0.1` reinstalled from the pinned versions; `pandas` restored to
`2.3.3`) and verified via the full test suite: 1776 passed, 23 skipped,
0 failed -- identical to the pre-attempt count, confirming no lasting
damage. SPARCL remains a real option only via a numpy-2-compatible future
release, or an isolated environment separate from this project's own
dependencies -- neither pursued. DESI spectrum retrieval (as opposed to the
catalog) is therefore STILL an open [GAP] -- Data Lab does not serve DESI
spectra at all (confirmed in the original session), and SPARCL is the only
documented route to them.

Session 2 tested `/tap/async` directly (real HTTP round trips against NOIRLab
Data Lab, not a guess) and found the actual fix, added as reusable
infrastructure in `tap.py` (`async_query`, `netclient.post`), not DESI-
specific code. Two SEPARATE problems had been conflated under the original
"sync times out" finding: (1) `WHERE targetid = <value>` and a plain
`BETWEEN` box both genuinely time out via `/tap/sync` but complete via
`/tap/async` in under a second (exact-match) to ~20s (box) -- confirmed
live, the sync endpoint's connection-timeout window, not query execution
speed, was the actual blocker. (2) `CONTAINS(POINT(...), CIRCLE(...))`
fails with the IDENTICAL `PSQLException: function point(...) does not
exist` via BOTH sync and async -- a real, permanent Postgres schema gap on
this specific table (`des_dr2.main` supports the identical ADQL shape
fine), not a timeout, and async cannot fix it.

`tap.async_query` implements the real, live-verified UWS (IVOA Universal
Worker Service) protocol: `POST {service}/async` -> follow the `303` to the
job's own status URL -> `POST {job_url}/phase` with `PHASE=RUN` (a fresh job
starts `PENDING`, not auto-run) -> poll `GET {job_url}/phase` until a
terminal phase -> on `COMPLETED`, parse the real `<uws:result
xlink:href="...">` and fetch it; on `ERROR`, surface the real
`<uws:errorSummary>` message. `surveys/desi.py`'s `cone_search` now builds a
bounding-box `BETWEEN` query (not `CONTAINS`/`CIRCLE`) via `_bounding_box`
(handling RA 0/360 wraparound and the near-pole `cos(dec)`-blowup case
explicitly), submits it through `async_query`, and applies an EXACT
great-circle filter (`crossmatch.angular_separation_arcsec`, reused
unchanged) to the returned rows before building `SourceRef`s -- verified
live end-to-end: a real cone search at RA=180, Dec=0 returned real DESI
targets, all confirmed within the exact requested radius (max offset
1793.97" for an 1800" request).

A second real, load-bearing bug was found and fixed while verifying this
live: `tap.py`'s existing `_cell()` cell-parser routed every numeric-looking
value through `float()` first, which silently loses precision for any
integer beyond float64's exact `2**53` range -- DESI `targetid`s are
~3.96e16, well past it. A live query for a specific real targetid came back
with a DIFFERENT number (off in the last two digits) purely from this round
trip, confirmed and reproduced directly (`float(39628379988689587) ==
39628379988689584.0`). This bug predates this session and affects EVERY
TAP-based connector's integer columns (`des.py`, `exoplanet_archive.py`),
not just DESI -- fixed by trying an exact `int(text)` parse first (Python
ints are exact and arbitrary-precision) before ever falling back to `float`.

Like every other opt-in research module in this file, NOT wired into
`rpc.py`, `scoring.WEIGHTS`, or `evidence.py`.
Tests: `tests/test_tap.py` (extended with 8 new cases: the large-integer
precision regression, a full mocked async job flow including a PENDING-
job start, polling through EXECUTING, the real error-message surfacing
case, a job that never reaches a terminal phase within `max_wait_seconds`,
and the non-`/sync`-URL rejection case -- which itself caught a real bug,
`_async_service_url`'s error originally escaping the surrounding try/except
uncaught), and `tests/test_surveys_desi.py` (fully rewritten: 21 cases for
`_bounding_box`'s wraparound/pole/clamping behaviour, the exact-radius
filter including a regression case for a row inside the bounding box but
outside the true circle, pagination, and one `live`-marked end-to-end case
-- this connector's first ever, now that the round trip is confirmed to
work). Full suite: 1785 passed, 24 skipped, 0 failed.

          LAMOST, HST/JWST real spectral-product download (as opposed to
          their existing metadata-only connectors), and a possible SPARCL-
          based DESI rewrite are explicit, open [GAP]s carried into the next
          session, not attempted here -- consistent with the "verify live
          before coding" discipline this codebase already follows, none of
          the three had a live-confirmed access path checked this session.

          Like every other opt-in research module in this file, NOT wired
          into `rpc.py`, `scoring.WEIGHTS`, or `evidence.py`.
          Tests: `tests/test_spectroscopy_calibration.py` (31 cases: line-
          candidate detection including the pure-noise and edge-effect
          regression cases, redshift recovery from single and multi-line
          synthetic injections including the empty-rest-line-dict and
          custom-rest-line-list cases, continuum-smoothness behaviour on
          smooth vs. discontinuous continua, the combined calibration
          report, and a static `rpc.py`-non-reference check), `tests/

          accurately injected multi-line redshifts, unresolved-spectra
