FOLLOW-UP: GAPS CLOSED FROM ITEMS 26-30'S OWN "WHAT'S LEFT" TABLE
                                                        <-- NEW 2026-08-26
---------------------------------------------------------------------

After items 26-30 landed, the user asked which of that batch's own
named gaps were actually implementable now versus genuinely blocked.
Of the ones marked implementable, the user asked for all of them except
wiring the batch into `rpc.py`/`scoring.WEIGHTS`/`evidence.py` (declined,
consistent with every module in this file staying opt-in) and running
real production-scale studies for all five items (scoped down to: build
the standing Kepler/K2 connector gap, plus run ONE real study with it).

[GAP CLOSED] Kepler/K2 survey connector
          `surveys/kepler.py` (`KeplerConnector`) is the concrete
          `lightkurve`-based Kepler/K2 fetch this file has named as open
          since items 16/18 (transit vetting, flare energy), following
          `surveys/tess.py`'s exact shape (same dedup/cadence/error-
          estimation pattern; `mission="Kepler"` or `"K2"` drives one
          connector instead of two near-duplicates). `enabled_by_default
          = False` -- unlike TESS, which is one of the three original
          default-enabled surveys, Kepler is added opt-in like every
          other connector this multi-session plan has added, so it does
          not silently change what `acquire`'s default survey list pulls
          for existing projects (a real regression caught by the full
          test suite: `tests/test_connectors.py`'s hardcoded default-
          enabled list, not by this module's own tests).
          A real bug was found and fixed via a live fetch this session:
          `meta.get("QUARTER") or fallback` treats Kepler's real Quarter
          0 (the mission's short initial commissioning quarter) as falsy,
          mislabelling a real Quarter-0 product "kepler-qunknown" instead
          of "kepler-q0" -- confirmed against KIC 9726699's own real
          Quarter 0 FITS product, fixed with an explicit `is not None`
          check.
          The real demonstration study: KIC 9726699 (GJ 1243), one of the
          best-studied Kepler flare stars in the literature (Hawley et
          al. 2014, ApJ 797, 121), fetched live across 6 real quarters
          (473 to 45254 points each, Quarters 0/1/2/3/6). `flare.
          detect_flare_candidates` (built in an earlier session, item 18)
          found 24 real flare candidates across those quarters on this
          real data -- directionally consistent with GJ 1243's known
          status as a frequent flarer, not a synthetic check. `flare.
          fit_flare_template`'s least-squares refinement, by contrast,
          did NOT converge cleanly on the specific real candidate windows
          tried this session (residual_rms far above 1, `n_evaluations=2`
          suggesting an early/degenerate termination) -- reported here
          honestly as a real, surfaced limitation of that pre-existing
          function on real (not synthetic) data, not silently worked
          around or hidden. `fit_flare_template` itself is unchanged;
          debugging its real-data convergence behaviour is out of scope
          for this session and remains open.
          Tests: `tests/test_surveys_kepler.py` (11 offline cases,
          including a regression test for the Quarter-0 bug, + 1 live
          case fetching KIC 9726699).

[GAP CLOSED] Item 26 -- absolute luminosity for parallax-bearing candidates
          `sed_energy_balance.absolute_luminosity_proxy` adds the real
          `4*pi*d^2` factor `energy_balance_residual`'s ratio-only design
          deliberately avoided, for the subset of candidates that DO have
          a real Gaia parallax. Verified: luminosity scales as distance^2
          for fixed apparent flux (the direct reason a distance is
          needed at all), and rejects non-positive parallax rather than
          fabricating a distance.

[GAP CLOSED] Item 26 -- class calibration on real labels
          `sed_energy_balance.evaluate_real_class_separability` replaces
          the synthetic class-conditional check with real ALeRCE-broker-
          classified objects (`class=AGN`/`class=YSO`, both confirmed
          live to return real rows) cross-matched against real 2MASS+WISE
          photometry near each object's real position, feeding this
          module's own `energy_balance_residual`. A real, honest finding
          from running this against real data: a first version using
          WISE alone always returned zero usable matches, because
          `energy_balance_residual` requires at least two bands in EACH
          of its uv_optical/dust_ir groups, and WISE alone only ever
          supplies one uv_optical-group band (W1) -- fixed by also
          querying 2MASS for J/H/K. RUN LIVE this session (30 real
          objects, 15 AGN + 15 YSO, 21 train / 9 test): macro-F1 = 0.6494,
          a real, meaningfully-above-chance (0.5 for a balanced binary)
          separation using real cross-survey photometry, not a synthetic
          number.

[GAP CLOSED] Item 27 -- quantitative validation against a real star
          A real Gaia DR3 source (4056453296603930624; found live this
          session via an ADQL query for parallax_over_error > 20,
          0.3 < ag_gspphot < 1.5, near the Galactic plane -- ra=268.196,
          dec=-29.655, parallax=2.819+-0.126 mas, ag_gspphot=0.834 mag)
          is run through the full real pipeline: `dust_3d.
          marginalize_extinction` against the real downloaded cube gives
          mean_extinction=0.733 mag; Gaia's own independent `ag_gspphot`
          estimate (new `surveys/gaia.query_extinction_estimate`) is
          0.834 mag -- a real residual of only -0.101 mag between two
          independent real methods for the same real star, closing the
          "qualitative sanity check only" caveat this item's own entry
          above named as open.
          Tests: `tests/test_surveys_gaia.py` (+3 cases) and
          `tests/test_dust_3d.py` (+1 live case running the full real
          comparison).

[GAP CLOSED] Item 27 -- finer-resolution dust cubes
          `dust_3d.fetch_dust_cube` now takes a `resolution_pc` argument
          ("010"/"025"/"050", default "050") selecting among the same
          Vergely+2022 cube family's three correlation-length variants --
          real file sizes confirmed live via HEAD requests this session
          (010pc: 232,617,600 bytes; 025pc: 117,034,560 bytes; 050pc:
          41,169,600 bytes, matching the already-validated default), each
          with its own byte cap rather than reusing one generic limit.

[GAP CLOSED] Item 28 -- broader polarimetry coverage
          `polarization_features.query_optical_polarization_secondary`
          adds a second real, independent optical polarimetry catalogue
          -- "Linear polarization of nearby bright stars" (Bailey, Hough
          & Young 2010, MNRAS 405, 2570), found live via
          `Vizier.find_catalogs` this session -- alongside the existing
          Santos+2011 sample. NOT merged into the same `p_percent` scale:
          a live sample row this session (Q/I=-34.0, U/I=13.8, Pol=36.7)
          confirms `Pol=hypot(Q/I,U/I)` internally, but the catalogue's
          own real-world unit convention was not independently confirmed
          against the source paper, so it is exposed under its own raw
          field names via `query_optical_polarization_any` (tries
          Santos+2011 first, falls back to Bailey+2010) rather than risk
          a silently wrong percent conversion.

[GAP CLOSED] Item 29 -- two-image ("double") lens systems
          `strong_lens.fit_lens_model` now accepts `observed_flux_ratios`
          -- the standard, literature "flux-ratio anomaly" technique
          (Mao & Schneider 1998; Dalal & Kochanek 2002) that gives a
          double's 4 position residuals the 5th constraint its 5 unknowns
          need. Needed a new `magnification()` function (`mu=1/det(A)`,
          `A` the lens-mapping Jacobian, via a central finite difference
          of the already-analytic `total_deflection` -- numerical
          differentiation of a closed-form field, not an approximate
          model). Verified: a real double built from a known SIE
          (source at beta=[0.6,0]) refit with its own TRUE flux ratio as
          the extra constraint converges to residual_rms < 1e-3.

[GAP CLOSED] Item 29 -- external shear
          `strong_lens.ExternalShear`/`shear_deflection`/`shear_potential`
          add the standard tidal-field extension (e.g. Kochanek 2006
          Saas-Fee notes) to the single-galaxy SIE model. A real
          subtlety, caught before it became a bug: external shear's
          deflection is DEGREE-1 homogeneous in theta (linear), unlike
          the SIE's degree-0 deflection, so `lensing_potential`'s
          `psi=theta.alpha` shortcut does NOT apply to it -- Euler's
          theorem instead gives `psi_shear=theta.alpha_shear/2` for a
          degree-1 field, implemented and verified via the same
          numerical-gradient check the SIE potential already uses.
          `solve_image_positions`/`total_deflection`/`total_potential`
          take an optional `shear` parameter; the existing SIE-only
          functions are unchanged.
          Tests: `tests/test_strong_lens.py` (+8 cases: shear potential/
          deflection consistency, magnification sign/decay, and the
          flux-ratio-constrained double recovery).

[GAP CLOSED] Item 30 -- real halo-mass inference
          `weak_lensing.py` gained the standard closed-form projected NFW
          profile (Wright & Brainerd 2000, ApJ 534, 34, eq. 11-14):
          `nfw_delta_sigma`/`nfw_tangential_shear` (the model),
          `nfw_enclosed_mass` (closed-form 3-D enclosed mass, NFW 1997),
          and `fit_nfw_halo_mass` (least-squares recovery of `(rho_s,
          r_s)` against an observed tangential-shear profile). This
          answers `environment_density_shear_correlation`'s own stated
          limit ("correlation only, no halo mass inferred") with a real
          mass estimate. Verified: a clean synthetic profile recovers
          injected `(rho_s, r_s)` to within 5%/10%; a profile with 2%
          Gaussian noise still recovers the true enclosed mass within
          20%; the piecewise `f(x)`/`h(x)` formulas are confirmed smooth
          across their `x=1` removable singularity, not discontinuous.
          Tests: `tests/test_weak_lensing.py` (+10 cases).

Like every other opt-in research addition in this file, none of this
follow-up work is wired into `rpc.py`/`scoring.WEIGHTS`/`evidence.py` --
the user explicitly declined that change for this batch. Full offline
suite re-run after all follow-up items landed: 1936 passed, 37 skipped,
0 failed.

Continuing the same "which named gaps are actually implementable"

[GAP CLOSED] Item 27 -- finer dust cubes actually downloaded and parsed
          Round 1 only HEAD-verified the "010"/"025" pc cube URLs and
          sizes. Both are now actually fetched and loaded live this
          session: "025" is a real (601, 601, 81) cube, step_pc=10.0;
          "010" is a real (601, 601, 161) cube, step_pc=5.0, 232.6 MB
          downloaded and parsed fully finite, no truncation.
          Tests: `tests/test_dust_3d.py` (+2 live cases).

[GAP CLOSED] Item 28 -- a second, much larger real RM catalogue
          `polarization_features.query_rotation_measure_nvss` adds
          Taylor, Stil & Sunstrum (2009, ApJ 702, 1230) -- the real,
          37,543-source all-sky NVSS RM catalogue widely cited in the
          literature as "the" NVSS RM catalogue, found live this session
          via a direct VizieR-ID lookup after `Vizier.find_catalogs` text
          search did not surface it under several phrasings tried.
          `query_rotation_measure_any` tries it first, falling back to
          the smaller Xu+2014 sample -- confirmed live to actually return
          real matching rows within a 1-degree cone at a generic sky
          position, unlike either smaller catalogue alone.

[GAP CLOSED] Item 28 -- Bailey+2010's real unit convention confirmed
          Round 1 exposed Bailey+2010's `Pol`/`Q/I`/`U/I` under raw field
          names specifically because their real-world units were
          unconfirmed. Confirmed live this session via the arXiv API
          (real title/authors: "The linear polarization of nearby bright
          stars measured at the parts per million level", Bailey, Lucas
          & Hough 2010, MNRAS 405, 2570, abs/1003.1753): the paper's own
          abstract states "BS 3982 (Regulus) has a polarization of
          ~37x10^-6"; the catalogue's real `HR=3982` row (HR and BS are
          the same Bright Star/Harvard Revised numbering) has `Pol=36.7`,
          confirming the units are 1e-6 (parts per million), not percent
          or 1e-4 as earlier guessed. `query_optical_polarization_
          secondary` now returns a real `p_percent`/`theta_deg` on the
          same scale `query_optical_polarization` (Santos+2011) already
          uses, alongside the raw `pol_raw_ppm` field, so `query_
          optical_polarization_any`'s two possible sources now share one
          real, confirmed schema instead of silently differing.

[GAP CLOSED] Item 28 -- real-label class separability
          `polarization_features.evaluate_real_class_separability` is
          the real-data counterpart to the synthetic `evaluate_class_
          separability`: real ALeRCE-classified AGN/YSO objects (same two
          classes `sed_energy_balance`'s real study already validated
          live), each contributing a real NVSS RM match (`|RM|`, 0.0 when
          genuinely absent) and a real ALeRCE-fetched ZTF light curve's
          own photometric scatter. RUN LIVE this session (30 real
          objects, 15 AGN + 15 YSO): macro-F1 = 0.25, reported honestly
          rather than tuned to look better -- a real, informative
          finding, not a success story: `n_no_rm_match=30` (every single
          one of the 30 real objects had NO real NVSS match within 120
          arcsec), meaning the RM feature carried zero real signal in
          this run and the classifier was left with only one informative
          real feature (photometric amplitude), which alone was not
          enough to separate these two classes above chance for this
          population. This is a genuinely different, less fortunate
          result than `sed_energy_balance`'s AGN/YSO study (macro-F1 =
          0.6494) using a different real feature set on the same two
          classes -- both are reported as they actually ran.
          Tests: `tests/test_polarization_features.py` (+8 offline cases
          for the NVSS catalogue functions, +2 live cases).

[GAP CLOSED] Item 26 -- absolute-luminosity proxy validated against a real star
          `absolute_luminosity_proxy` was previously only checked for
          correct distance^2 scaling. Real Gaia DR3 source
          4056453296603930624 (the same star item 27's dust cross-check
          uses) has real `radius_gspphot`/`teff_gspphot` (0.740 Rsun /
          3681 K), giving a real, independent Stefan-Boltzmann reference
          luminosity via `flare_energy.quiescent_bolometric_luminosity`
          (already verified against the nominal solar constant in an
          earlier session). Real 2MASS J/H/K photometry for the same star
          (12.871/12.985/11.756, confirmed live -- no WISE counterpart
          was found within 6 arcsec, a real coverage gap, not fabricated)
          feeds the proxy. Result: proxy = 1.473e32 erg/s vs. Stefan-
          Boltzmann = 3.470e32 erg/s, ratio ~0.42 -- same order of
          magnitude, not off by orders of magnitude, which is the
          expected, honest behaviour of a coarse 3-band trapezoidal
          nu*F_nu integral against a star's TRUE bolometric flux (most of
          which sits shortward of J or in unsampled bands): a real
          quantitative confirmation that "coarse proxy" (this function's
          own docstring) is an accurate self-description, not an
          understatement or overstatement.
          Tests: `tests/test_sed_energy_balance.py` (+1 live case).

[GAP CLOSED] Item 30 -- real KiDS-450 data validation
          DES was checked live this session and confirmed to publish no
          VizieR-reachable per-galaxy shear/shape catalogue (only DR1/DR2
          co-add photometry) -- neither `calibrate_shear_bias` nor
          `tangential_shear_profile` had ever run against ANY real shear
          data before this round. 200 real KiDS-450 sources fetched live
          around a generic field (RA=180, Dec=0): real `e1`/`e2` are
          bounded in [-1, 1] with a near-zero mean (mean e1=0.0009, mean
          e2=0.0077 -- no coherent lensing signal expected at a random,
          non-cluster field, which is exactly what was found), and real
          `MultCal` (KiDS's own published per-object multiplicative
          calibration) has mean=-0.081, std=0.208, consistent in sign and
          rough magnitude with KiDS-450's own published calibration study
          (Fenech Conti et al. 2017, MNRAS 467, 1627). `tangential_
          shear_profile` was confirmed to run end-to-end on these real
          e1/e2/position values and return finite results. This is a
          real-data INTEGRATION check (the pipeline runs correctly on
          real archival shear data), explicitly not a real cluster-mass
          detection -- finding a specific real cluster confirmed to fall
          inside KiDS-450's actual observed pointings was not attempted
          this session and remains open.
          Tests: `tests/test_weak_lensing.py` (+2 live cases).

[GAP CLOSED, PRE-EXISTING BUG] `flare.fit_flare_template` real
          convergence bug, found via the Kepler/K2 connector's own GJ
          1243 validation study
          Round 1 reported this honestly as an unresolved real-data
          limitation without touching `flare.py` (out of that round's
          scope). Investigated and fixed this round: `scipy.optimize.
          least_squares`'s default `xtol` (relative step-size)
          termination compares each fitted parameter's step against ITS
          OWN magnitude. Kepler's absolute BJD_TDB time values are
          ~2.455e6 (days), so with `t_peak` at that scale and `fwhm`/
          `amplitude` at ~1e-2/1e-1, the optimizer falsely reported
          `xtol` convergence after exactly 2 function evaluations without
          ever correcting the initial guess -- confirmed directly by
          reproducing the exact real failure outside the fit function
          (cost frozen at 606,723 across both evaluations). Invisible in
          this function's own prior synthetic tests because those used
          small, near-zero relative time values where the scale mismatch
          never triggers -- a real, previously-undetected latent bug in a
          function shipped and "validated" in an earlier session, only
          surfaced by feeding it genuinely real (not synthetic) data for
          the first time.
          Fix: `fit_flare_template` now fits in time RELATIVE TO THE
          INITIAL `t_peak` GUESS (so `t_peak` starts the optimization at
          0, the same order of magnitude as `fwhm`/`amplitude`) and
          shifts the fitted `t_peak` back to absolute time afterward.
          Confirmed against the exact real data that exposed the bug:
          31 evaluations (not 2), real `ftol` convergence (not a false
          `xtol` one). Re-ran this round's own GJ 1243 study end to end
          with the fix: all 24 real flare candidates across 6 real
          quarters now successfully fit (0/24 did before), with
          `n_evaluations` genuinely ranging 16-33 across them. Residual
          RMS values remain large in chi units for several of them (a
          real, expected consequence of Kepler-precision photometry
          combined with a single-peak template against real, possibly
          multi-component real flares -- NOT the same failure as the
          bug just fixed, since the fit now genuinely iterates and
          converges rather than freezing at the initial guess).
          Tests: `tests/test_flare.py` (+1 regression case reproducing
          the exact Kepler-scale failure mode and asserting
          `n_evaluations > 2`); `tests/test_flare_energy.py` (existing
          suite re-run, unaffected).

[STILL OPEN, CHECKED THIS ROUND] Item 29 -- real recall/precision study
          for strong lensing
          Checked live this session: no VizieR-reachable catalogue of
          real multiply-imaged quasar/galaxy IMAGE POSITIONS (as opposed
          to just lens-candidate sky positions, which `J/A+A/688/A34`
          already provides) was found under several search phrasings
          (`Vizier.find_catalogs` for CASTLES, Sluse+2003/RXJ1131,
          PG1115+080, Q2237+0305 astrometry). A real per-system astrometry
          reference is what `fit_lens_model` would need to validate
          against a real, literature-confirmed lens rather than a
          synthetic one -- deliberately not attempted from memory (this
          codebase's own "verified, not assumed" discipline), so this
          stays explicitly open rather than filled with unverified
          numbers.

[STILL OPEN, CHECKED THIS ROUND] Item 30 -- real cluster-mass detection
          `fit_nfw_halo_mass` remains validated only against synthetic
          NFW profiles and the real-but-generic-field KiDS-450 sanity
          check above. Finding a specific real, published galaxy cluster
          confirmed to fall inside one of KiDS-450's actual DR3 survey
          pointings (not just anywhere in its nominal ~1350 sq deg
          footprint) was not attempted this round and remains open.

FOLLOW-UP (ROUND 3): LARGE-SCALE REAL STUDIES FOR 27/29/30

Round 2 closed single-object/small-N real checks for several items but
left large-scale (many-object/many-field) real studies open for items
27, 29 and 30. This round closes those.

[GAP CLOSED] Item 27 -- population-scale real extinction recovery
          `dust_3d.evaluate_extinction_recovery_real` runs the full real
          pipeline (fetch cube -> marginalize -> compare to Gaia's own
          `ag_gspphot`) across a whole real Gaia DR3 sample instead of
          one star. RUN LIVE this session: 60 real stars
          (parallax_over_error>15, 0.05<ag_gspphot<2.5, |b|<5 deg, a real
          ADQL query), 60/60 used, 0 failed. Real result, reported
          honestly rather than cherry-picked: mean_residual=-0.296 mag,
          median=-0.381, std=0.363, mean_absolute_residual=0.394 -- a
          real, larger systematic offset than the single lucky star
          checked in round 2 (-0.10 mag) suggested. At population scale,
          this dust map's A0(550nm) reads systematically LOWER than
          Gaia's own `ag_gspphot` by roughly a third of a magnitude on
          average, with real scatter around that offset -- a genuine,
          informative population-level finding, not a contradiction of
          the earlier single-star check (both are real; the single star
          was simply closer to the mean than a random draw would be).
          Tests: `tests/test_dust_3d.py` (+3 offline synthetic cases,
          +1 live case that re-queries Gaia fresh each run).

[GAP CLOSED] Item 30 -- multi-field real density-shear correlation
          `weak_lensing.evaluate_environment_density_shear_correlation_
          real` runs `environment_density_shear_correlation` across
          several real KiDS-450 fields instead of one. KiDS-450 is
          patchy (confirmed live: scanning a 45-point RA/Dec grid found
          real coverage at only 6 points), so 9 real field centres were
          located live this session by that same scan. RUN LIVE: 9 real
          fields, 300-arcsec cones, pearson_r=0.3463 between real local
          source density and real innermost-bin tangential shear
          amplitude -- reported honestly as a weak correlation from only
          9 GENERIC field centres (none targeted at a known cluster),
          consistent with expected noise rather than a real detected
          halo-environment signal, not oversold as a detection.
          Tests: `tests/test_weak_lensing.py` (+2 offline cases, +1 live
          case).

[GAP CLOSED, REAL BUG FOUND] Item 29 -- population-scale real multi-
          survey coverage study (recall/precision itself remains blocked)
          No live-reachable catalogue of real multi-image lens astrometry
          was found even after a further search this round, so the real
          recall/precision study stays explicitly open (see round 2's
          entry). What IS real and reachable: `strong_lens.
          evaluate_multi_survey_coverage_real` cross-matches real KiDS
          DR4 strong-lens CANDIDATE positions (not recall/precision --
          just "does independent imaging exist to vet this candidate
          against") against DES and Pan-STARRS. The real candidate list
          (564 entries) was fetched live via VizieR's plain (non-cone-
          search) VOTable endpoint after `tap.parse_votable` was tried
          first and found, live this session, to return every field as
          `None` against that endpoint's multi-`<TABLE>` structure -- a
          real, narrow limitation of that shared parser, worked around
          with a plain-regex fallback local to this one real study
          rather than widened, under this round's time constraints
          (three other real connectors already depend on that parser's
          current behaviour).
          A SECOND, more significant real bug was found running this
          study live: `surveys/des.py`'s `cone_search` (built and only
          mock-tested in an earlier session) currently raises a bare
          `KeyError` against the real NOIRLab Data Lab TAP service --
          confirmed by reproducing the exact query directly: the ADQL
          `CONTAINS(POINT('ICRS',...), CIRCLE('ICRS',...))` cone-search
          construct DES's own `build_query` emits now fails server-side
          with `PSQLException: ERROR: function point(unknown, double
          precision, double precision) does not exist`, and `des.py`'s
          `parse_csv` has no handling for a TAP ERROR response, so the
          error VOTable's XML gets misparsed as CSV and the resulting
          row has no `coadd_object_id` key, crashing the pagination
          loop. Two quick alternative ADQL cone-search forms (`q3c_
          radial_query`, an explicit `'t'=CONTAINS(...)` cast) were tried
          live and also failed -- the real, correct fix for DES's own
          ADQL dialect was not found this session and DES's `cone_search`
          is left UNCHANGED (fixing it blind, without confirming the
          real working syntax, risks shipping an equally wrong fix).
          Instead, `evaluate_multi_survey_coverage_real` wraps each
          connector call in its own try/except (the same "one bad X does
          not abort the batch" discipline `surveys/kepler.py`'s per-
          quarter handling already uses), counting real connector
          failures separately (`n_des_errors`/`n_panstarrs_errors`)
          rather than silently folding them into "no match". RUN LIVE
          this session against 50 real KiDS candidates: DES failed on
          all 50 (the bug above, now visible as `n_des_errors=50` rather
          than a crash or a silent false "no coverage" count);
          Pan-STARRS succeeded and returned a real, non-degenerate
          coverage fraction.
          => `surveys/des.py`'s `cone_search` is now a confirmed-broken
          real connector against the live service, not merely
          "unvalidated" as its own docstring previously said -- this is
          a real regression/finding for a future session to fix, not
          fixed here.
          Tests: `tests/test_strong_lens.py` (+2 offline cases, +1 live
          case exercising the real candidate fetch + real cross-match +
          real DES failure handling).

[GAP CLOSED, REAL BUG FOUND] Item 30 -- real cluster-mass detection
          The gap round 2 explicitly left open: a specific real, published
          cluster confirmed inside KiDS-450's actual pointings. Found live
          this session via `Vizier.find_catalogs` -> `J/ApJS/224/1`
          (redMaPPer DES cluster catalogue, Rykoff et al. 2016, ApJS 224,
          1), cross-checked against the 9 real-coverage field centres
          round 3's density-shear study already located: real cluster
          ID 1413 (richness Ng=140, RA=140.3977149, Dec=-0.2415848,
          z_lambda=0.3403) falls inside the real (140, 0) KiDS-450 field.
          Real KiDS-450 sources within 300 arcsec (500 fetched, 220 real
          background sources with `z_best > z_cluster+0.2`), a real
          critical surface density from `astropy.cosmology.Planck18` at
          the cluster's real redshift and the real background sample's
          median redshift, and `fit_nfw_halo_mass` against the real
          binned tangential-shear profile gave a real, finite mass
          (~4.6e13 Msun within 1 Mpc) -- on the low side of, but not
          implausible for, a richness-140 cluster, given real noise in a
          several-hundred-source public catalogue (several bins show
          real negative/non-monotonic shear -- genuine measurement
          noise, not a clean profile) and `r_s` pinning near its fit
          bound, an honest sign this one real cluster alone does not
          tightly constrain both NFW parameters (the same real limitation
          any single, non-stacked real cluster weak-lensing measurement
          has with a modest source count).
          A REAL BUG was found and fixed getting here: the first attempt
          (a wide 900-arcsec cone, mostly empty inner bins) drove
          `fit_nfw_halo_mass`'s unbounded `method="lm"` search to
          physically absurd `(rho_s, r_s)` values, overflowing `exp()`
          and returning `enclosed_mass_msun=inf` -- confirmed directly,
          not assumed. Fixed by bounding the fit (`rho_s` in
          1e8-1e20 Msun/Mpc^3, `r_s` in 0.01-20 Mpc, generous enough to
          cover any physically realistic halo) and switching to
          `method="trf"` (the same `"lm"` -> `"trf"` precedent
          `strong_lens.fit_lens_model` already set for exactly this
          "bounds needed" reason) -- confirmed to keep the existing
          synthetic recovery tests passing while making the real,
          previously-blowing-up cluster fit finite and well-behaved.
          Tests: `tests/test_weak_lensing.py` (+1 regression case
          reproducing the overflow with a small noisy synthetic profile,
          +1 live case running the full real cluster study end to end).

Full offline suite re-run after this round's items landed: 1950 passed,
48 skipped, 0 failed.

FOLLOW-UP (ROUND 4): PIXEL-LEVEL STRONG-LENS IMAGING FIT
                                                        <-- NEW 2026-08-26
---------------------------------------------------------------------

The one item left genuinely open as "buildable, but a big separate
project" -- everything in `strong_lens.py` fits isolated IMAGE POSITIONS,
never real pixels. Built this round as a new module, `strong_lens_
imaging.py`, the same "forward model fit to real pixel data" shape
`tess_psf.py` already established for TESS PSF deblending.

[GAP CLOSED] Pixel-level imaging fit
          Real image source: Pan-STARRS1, via its own public,
          credential-free cutout service (`ps1images.stsci.edu`).
          DES was checked FIRST and confirmed broken this session (see
          the item-29 entry above); Hubble/JWST are narrow pointed
          programmes unlikely to cover a generic candidate; PS1 is the
          practical real, all-sky (dec>-30) choice. Confirmed live:
          `ps1filenames.py` (position -> real stacked-image filename)
          and `fitscut.cgi` (real FITS cutout) both work with no
          authentication -- a real 250 KB FITS cutout was fetched and
          its own header's `CDELT1/2` (6.944e-5 deg = 0.25 arcsec)
          confirmed PS1's real 0.25"/pixel plate scale directly, not
          from documentation.
          `render_lensed_image` ray-traces every image-plane pixel back
          through `strong_lens.total_deflection` (reused unchanged --
          SIE + optional external shear, both already built and verified
          in round 2) to a single elliptical GAUSSIAN source profile
          (`GaussianSource`), then convolves with a Gaussian PSF
          (`scipy.ndimage.gaussian_filter`, no new dependency).
          Deliberately not a full Sersic profile: a lensed source is
          typically compact and barely resolved even in good seeing, so
          Gaussian is a real, standard, much simpler starting model --
          the same "simpler real model over unneeded complexity" choice
          `transit_ttv.py` already made against a compiled transit-
          modelling dependency.
          `fit_pixel_lens_model` is the actual pixel-level least-squares
          fit (8 parameters: theta_e, axis_ratio, position_angle, source
          beta_x/beta_y/amplitude/scale_radius, background), verified on
          synthetic images: exact recovery (theta_e/axis_ratio to 5-10%)
          on a noiseless rendered image, and the right order of magnitude
          under realistic Gaussian pixel noise (0.3 counts/pixel).
          RUN LIVE this session against a real cutout at the same real
          redMaPPer cluster centroid item 30's cluster-mass study used
          (RA=140.3977, Dec=-0.2416): the pipeline ran end to end and
          returned finite numbers, but the fit landed on a degenerate
          "effectively no lensing" solution (`theta_e~21"`, larger than
          the whole 15" cutout; `axis_ratio~0.002`, pinned near its
          lower bound) -- reported HONESTLY as the real, expected result:
          this is a cluster centroid used for a mass estimate, never a
          confirmed strong-lens system, so a real absence of a lensing
          signal in its central galaxy's light is the physically correct
          outcome, not a bug or a failed detection. Finding and fitting a
          real CONFIRMED multiply-imaged system's real cutout (which
          would need identifying the exact real image separations first
          -- the same real-astrometry gap round 2's item-29 entry
          already named as blocked) remains open.
          Like every other opt-in research module in this file, NOT
          wired into `rpc.py`/`scoring.WEIGHTS`/`evidence.py`.
          Tests: `tests/test_strong_lens_imaging.py` (13 offline cases
          + 2 live cases: a real cutout fetch/load, and the real
          end-to-end pixel-fit run described above).

Full offline suite re-run after this round's items landed: 1963 passed,
50 skipped, 0 failed.

FOLLOW-UP (ROUND 5): TWO REAL BUGS FIXED
                                                        <-- NEW 2026-08-26
---------------------------------------------------------------------

[FIXED] `surveys/des.py`'s broken TAP cone search
          Diagnosed properly this round rather than left as a known
          regression. Confirmed live: the standard ADQL geometry cone
          search fails SERVICE-WIDE (reproduced against a second,
          unrelated table, `ls_dr9.tractor`, same error) -- the current
          NOIRLab Data Lab TAP backend is missing whatever Postgres
          extension (e.g. pg_sphere) its ADQL-to-SQL translation needs
          for `point()`/`circle()`. Not a client-side syntax problem:
          `LANG=SQL`/`Postgres`/`postgresql` are all rejected
          (`unknown LANG`), so only ADQL is available, and no ADQL
          rephrasing (an explicit `1=CONTAINS(...)` cast, `q3c_radial_
          query`) fixed it either -- both tried live, both failed.
          The real, working fix: a plain rectangular `ra`/`dec` `BETWEEN`
          bounding box, no geometry functions at all -- confirmed live to
          return real DES DR2 rows in ~4 seconds. Since a box is a
          SQUARE region, `cone_search` now post-filters to the real
          requested circular radius via `crossmatch.
          angular_separation_arcsec` (reused unchanged). A second, related
          bug was fixed alongside it: a TAP error response is itself a
          VOTable/XML document, not CSV, and used to be silently
          misparsed by `csv.DictReader` into a row with no
          `coadd_object_id` key, crashing pagination with a bare
          `KeyError` -- `_is_error_response` now detects that shape first
          and raises a clear `DESQueryError` instead.
          Re-ran round 3's real 50-candidate multi-survey coverage study
          with both this fix and the `parse_votable` fix below: DES now
          returns real (non-error) matches instead of `n_des_errors=50`.
          Tests: `tests/test_surveys_des.py` (existing 12 cases updated
          for the new bounding-box query shape and real circular post-
          filtering; +4 new cases: a bounding-box-corner rejection, a
          real-shaped TAP error response, and the box-centring math;
          +1 live case).

[FIXED] `tap.parse_votable`'s multi-`<TABLE>` desync
          The narrow parser gap round 3 worked around locally rather than
          fixed. Root cause, confirmed against the real 6-table, 564-row
          document that first exposed it: the original implementation
          collected every `<FIELD>` and every `<TR>` in the WHOLE
          document into two flat, DOCUMENT-WIDE lists, then zipped each
          row's cells against that single global field-name list by
          position -- with more than one `<TABLE>` present, a table's own
          `<TR>` values (as many cells as ITS OWN field count) got zipped
          against the wrong, differently-sized global list, so every
          returned row's fields came back `None`. Fixed by scoping both
          `fields` and the row-building loop to each `<TABLE>` in turn,
          concatenating rows across tables -- a no-op for the ordinary
          single-table case every existing caller (`vlass.py`,
          `strong_lens.py`, `weak_lensing.py`, `surveys/galex.py`,
          `surveys/herschel.py`, `surveys/twomass.py`, `surveys/wise.py`,
          this file's own TAP query path) already depends on and stayed
          green against. `tests/test_strong_lens.py`'s own
          `_fetch_real_kids_lens_candidates` (round 3's plain-regex
          workaround) now uses the fixed shared parser directly instead.
          Re-verified against the exact real document that exposed the
          bug: all 564 real rows now parse with populated fields, not
          `None`.
          Tests: `tests/test_tap.py` (+5 cases: single-table unchanged,
          a real 2-table desync regression, a 6-table shape matching the
          real document that exposed the bug, cross-table `limit`, and
          unparseable-XML error handling).

Full offline suite re-run after this round's items landed: 1971 passed,
51 skipped, 0 failed.

EXTERNAL ROADMAP ITEMS

[PARTIAL] Probabilistic host-galaxy association (P1, roadmap item 31)  <-- NEW 2026-08-26
          `host_association.py` (offset likelihood, Schechter luminosity
          prior, Bayesian combine) and `host_association_eval.py` (top-k
          recall + probability calibration studies, split purely for the
          500-line guideline, same `stellar_manifold.py`/
          `stellar_manifold_eval.py`/`agn_changepoint.py`/`agn_changepoint_
          eval.py` rationale) are this codebase's first host-association
          module -- genuinely greenfield: no host-galaxy, Schechter,
          luminosity-function, or `distance_modulus` code existed anywhere
          in `engine/astra` before this session (grepped, zero hits).

          Follows the PATH algorithm's structure (Gagliano et al. 2021,
          ApJ 908, 170) in its bounded, Sersic-fit-free fallback mode --
          this codebase has no image-based galaxy-profile-fitting stage,
          so each candidate is modelled as a single circular exponential
          light profile rather than a fitted Sersic ellipse, a real,
          stated scope limit (no ellipticity/position angle). The offset
          likelihood `f(r) = (r / r_e**2) * exp(-r / r_e)` is the closed-
          form radial marginal of that profile -- derived in the module's
          own docstring from `integral I(r)*2*pi*r dr = I0*2*pi*r_e**2`,
          and verified this session to integrate to exactly 1 via
          `scipy.integrate.quad` (`tests/test_host_association.py`), not
          merely asserted. The luminosity prior uses real, citable SDSS
          r-band Schechter parameters (Blanton et al. 2003, ApJ 592, 819,
          Table 2, `h=1` convention: `M*=-20.44`, `alpha=-1.05`), with only
          the shape term kept (no `phi*` normalization) since the module
          only ever ranks candidates relatively, never reports an absolute
          number density. A Gaia-cross-match parallax-SNR check
          (`surveys/gaia.py`'s existing `derived_properties`, reused
          unchanged) supplies a hard veto against a DES/Pan-STARRS
          detection actually being a foreground Galactic star rather than
          a galaxy.

          All data-access dependencies already existed as real, live-
          verified connectors except two small additive column changes,
          both made this session: `surveys/des.py`'s fixed ADQL SELECT
          gained `flux_radius` (a galaxy half-light-radius proxy from
          `des_dr2.main`) and `surveys/panstarrs.py`'s `cone_search` gained
          `rKronRad` -- both column names come from each archive's
          published documentation, NOT from a live query this session (no
          live network access at implementation time), the same
          "unverified until checked live" caveat every other connector
          column addition in this file states until confirmed; both
          modules' own docstrings now carry this caveat explicitly. A
          missing/malformed value degrades to `None` (`HostCandidate.
          r_e_arcsec`), which `associate_host` handles by falling back to
          `DEFAULT_R_E_ARCSEC`, a stated fallback assumption, never a
          fabricated measurement -- the same "stated missing, not
          fabricated" discipline `physical.characterize` already applies
          to missing photometric bands. `surveys/sdss.py`'s
          `query_spectroscopic_redshifts` and `surveys/desi.py`'s
          `DESIConnector` (both already real, live, opt-in connectors)
          supply candidate redshifts unchanged; when neither matches, the
          model degrades to offset-only association, again visibly (via
          `HostCandidate.redshift is None`) rather than silently.

          `associate_host` is the pure, offline, fully unit-testable
          Bayesian-combine function (no network calls -- redshift/size/
          foreground-flag evidence is passed in as plain dicts keyed by
          `SourceRef.object_id`); `find_and_associate_host` is a thin
          orchestration layer that actually calls DES/Pan-STARRS/SDSS/
          DESI/Gaia, kept deliberately separate so the math stays testable
          without mocking five connectors at once -- the same split
          `sn_classification.py`'s MCMC fit keeps from its ALeRCE-fetching
          caller. `find_and_associate_host` issues one SDSS + one DESI +
          one Gaia query PER CANDIDATE, bounded only by how many
          candidates a small search radius returns, not a batch endpoint
          -- acceptable for one-transient-at-a-time research use, not
          swept into any bulk pipeline, a real stated cost limitation.

          `host_association_eval.py` validates against SYNTHETIC ground
          truth only (the same "mechanism validated on synthetic data, not
          yet run at real Stage-B scale" caveat every eval module in this
          family states) -- a real top-k recall/calibration study needs a
          real catalog of transients with spectroscopically confirmed
          hosts, not attempted here. `synthesize_host_population` draws
          the true host's offset via `numpy.random.Generator.gamma(shape=
          2.0, scale=r_e)` rather than a hand-inverted CDF, since `f(r)`
          above IS exactly the Gamma(shape=2, scale=r_e) density (confirmed
          by direct comparison to its closed form); magnitudes are drawn
          from the Schechter prior via bounded rejection sampling over a
          fixed finite magnitude range, a real stated simplification (the
          unnormalized Schechter density's faint-end tail is not properly
          integrable without `phi*`, so sampling is only well-posed over a
          bounded range). `evaluate_top_k_recall` and `evaluate_
          probability_calibration` (an ECE-style reliability-curve binning)
          answer this backlog item's own named metrics directly.

          Like every other opt-in research module in this file, NOT wired
          into `rpc.py`, `scoring.WEIGHTS`, or `evidence.py`.
          Tests: `tests/test_host_association.py` (16 cases: offset-
          likelihood normalization/shape/validation, Schechter-prior and
          distance-modulus arithmetic, Bayesian-combine ranking correctness,
          offset-only degradation without a redshift, fallback-radius
          behavior with a missing size, foreground-star veto and the
          all-vetoed no-host case, empty-candidate-list handling, prior
          validation, `to_dict` shape, and a static `rpc.py`-non-reference
          check), `tests/test_host_association_eval.py` (9 cases: synthetic
          population shape/validation, top-k recall on a well-separated
          population and its monotonicity in `k`, calibration ECE/bin-
          coverage arithmetic, both modules' input validation, `to_dict`
          shapes, and a static `rpc.py`-non-reference check), and extended
          `tests/test_surveys_des.py`/`tests/test_surveys_panstarrs.py`
          covering the two new size-column reads (present/missing).

          UPDATE 2026-08-26 (2): the two column-name caveats this entry's
          first version stated as "unverified" were checked live this
          session and both turned up real, fixed bugs, not confirmations.
          DES: `des_dr2.main` has no bare `flux_radius` column at all --
          confirmed via a live `TAP_SCHEMA.columns` query -- only per-band
          `flux_radius_{g,i,r,y,z}`/`kron_radius`/`kron_radius_{...}`; and
          the real column's `unit` field (also read live) is `pixel`, not
          arcsec, confirmed sane by a live sample query (`flux_radius_r`
          values of ~1.5-4.2 at RA=45.0/Dec=-40.0, consistent with a few-
          pixel half-light radius). `surveys/des.py` now selects
          `flux_radius_r` and converts via a new `DES_PIXEL_SCALE_ARCSEC =
          0.263` constant (the standard, published DECam pixel scale --
          Abbott et al. 2018, ApJS 239, 18), storing the already-converted
          arcsec value under `flux_radius_r_arcsec`; a live end-to-end
          `cone_search` re-run against the same real field returned
          physically sane values (~0.65-0.71 arcsec half-light radii for
          two real faint DES galaxies). Pan-STARRS: the `mean` object
          endpoint `PanSTARRSConnector` queries was confirmed live to have
          NO Kron-radius or any other size/shape column at all (only
          `{band}MeanKronMag`, a magnitude) -- a real Kron-radius field
          (`{band}KronRad`) exists only on the separate `stack` endpoint,
          confirmed live the same session, which this connector does not
          query. This is a genuine, confirmed data-source gap, not merely
          an unverified assumption: the attempted `rKronRad` column
          addition was reverted, and `host_association.py`'s `_SIZE_KEYS`
          now only reads the DES arcsec column -- every Pan-STARRS-only
          host candidate falls back to `DEFAULT_R_E_ARCSEC`. Switching
          `PanSTARRSConnector` to the `stack` endpoint (a differently
          shaped, per-detection rather than per-object table) would be a
          real scope change to its existing `mean_photometry` contract,
          not attempted here. Full offline suite re-verified after these
          fixes: 1996 passed, 51 skipped, 0 failed.


[PARTIAL] Selection-function and population-rate inference (P1, roadmap item 32)  <-- NEW 2026-08-26
          `population_rate.py` (footprint/stratum types, the closed-form
          Poisson-Gamma rate estimator, the hierarchical MCMC rate fit,
          the anchor-survey sweep) and `population_rate_eval.py` (rate-
          posterior coverage + anchor-bias studies, split purely for the
          500-line guideline, same `stellar_manifold.py`/
          `stellar_manifold_eval.py` rationale) are this item's module
          pair. Unusually well set up by existing infrastructure compared
          to a typical greenfield item in this file: three of the four
          named inputs already had real, reusable machinery, confirmed by
          reading each fully before writing anything new.

          Injection recovery reuses `significance.evaluate_selection`/
          `fit_selection_model` UNCHANGED as the per-stratum completeness
          input, the same "thin wrapper" pattern `microlensing_eval.
          event_efficiency` already established for that module.
          `microlensing_fit.sample_posterior`'s exact `emcee` template
          (flat priors in bounds, walkers jittered around a starting
          guess, convergence via "chain >= 50 autocorrelation times,"
          emcee's own documented rule of thumb) is reused for the
          hierarchical fit below -- `emcee` needed no new dependency
          entry, already gated under `engine/pyproject.toml`'s `research`
          extra. `microlensing_eval.CoverageTrial`/`posterior_coverage`
          are reused UNCHANGED for the "coverage of simulated rate
          posteriors" metric this item names -- the exact same "simulate
          a truth, refit, count how often the credible interval contains
          it" mechanism that module already validated for microlensing
          parameters, applied here to `mu_log_rate` instead.
          `crossmatch.group_sources`/`grouping_bias_report`/
          `_resolve_anchor` are reused UNCHANGED for the "bias under
          anchor-survey changes" metric -- that module's own docstring
          already stated changing the anchor "controls the population
          denominator; it does not remove the underlying selection bias,"
          which `population_rate_eval.evaluate_anchor_survey_bias` now
          makes concrete and testable rather than just asserted in prose.

          The hierarchical model itself (genuinely new, confirmed
          greenfield: grepped for "Poisson"/"hierarchical" as population-
          inference code across `engine/astra`, zero hits) is a standard
          partial-pooling Poisson rate model in the non-centered
          parametrization (Gelman & Hill 2007, *Data Analysis Using
          Regression and Multilevel/Hierarchical Models*, Ch. 12;
          Betancourt & Girolami 2015 on non-centered parametrizations):
          per stratum `i`, `z_i ~ Normal(0,1)`, `rate_i = exp(mu_log_rate +
          sigma_log_rate * z_i)`, `expected_i = rate_i * exposure_i *
          completeness_i` (the standard Poisson-thinning identity --
          a completeness-thinned Poisson process is itself Poisson at the
          thinned rate), `detected_i ~ Poisson(expected_i)`. `mu_log_rate`/
          `sigma_log_rate` are the population-level parameters of
          interest; each `z_i` is a per-stratum nuisance parameter,
          `2 + n_strata` dimensions total -- a real, stated scope limit:
          sized for a handful of strata, not hundreds. Verified this
          session on synthetic strata with a known injected `mu_log_rate`:
          `fit_hierarchical_rate` recovers it to within 0.6 in log-rate
          (a real, if loose, tolerance -- `n_strata=8` strata is a small
          information budget for a 10-dimensional posterior) and all 21
          new tests, including the full MCMC recovery and coverage
          studies, passed on the first real run against a live `emcee`
          install.

          A second, closed-form, non-hierarchical estimator is included
          alongside the MCMC fit and is NOT novel: `poisson_rate_
          credible_interval` is the standard Jeffreys-prior conjugate
          Gamma posterior for a Poisson rate (`Gamma(detected + 1/2,
          scale=1/exposure)` -- the general conjugate-Gamma-for-Poisson-
          rate result, e.g. Gelman, Carlin, Stern & Rubin, *Bayesian Data
          Analysis*, 3rd ed., Ch. 2), verified in tests against a hand-
          computed `scipy.stats.gamma.ppf` quantile. Used both to seed the
          MCMC walkers (the same role a point-estimate fit plays in
          `microlensing_fit.py`) and, since it needs no sampler, as the
          fast per-anchor estimator inside `anchor_survey_rate_sweep`
          (rerun once per candidate anchor without a full MCMC each time).

          `population_rate_eval.evaluate_anchor_survey_bias` is designed
          to make the "bias under anchor-survey changes" metric concretely
          testable rather than a restated warning: it builds ONE synthetic
          multi-survey population observed by surveys with DIFFERENT known
          completeness values, then compares a "corrected" arm (each
          anchor's own raw group count divided by ITS OWN real
          completeness, via `anchor_survey_rate_sweep`) against a "naive"
          arm (every anchor's count divided by one SHARED assumed
          completeness -- the mistake a careless multi-survey analysis can
          make). Verified this session: the corrected arm's rate estimate
          stays close to the true injected rate for both anchor choices
          (`corrected_rate_by_anchor` within 50% of `true_rate_deg2_day`
          in the test), while the naive arm's spread across anchors is
          reliably larger than the corrected arm's spread
          (`naive_rate_spread > corrected_rate_spread`, asserted directly)
          -- a real, reproducible demonstration of the failure mode this
          item's metric names, not merely an assertion that `group_sources`
          "could" be biased.

          `SurveyFootprint` is deliberately a single effective sky area
          plus time baseline per survey, not a patchy-coverage HEALPix
          map -- `healpix_common.py` (already used by `gw.py`/`frb.py`/
          `kilonova_eval.py`) is noted as where a future full-coverage-map
          version would hang, not attempted here. No cadence-log data
          structure was built: `significance.evaluate_selection` already
          accepts `cadence_days` as a stratification dimension, so
          cadence's effect on completeness is captured by running
          injection-recovery stratified by `cadence_days` upstream of this
          module -- a deliberate reuse decision, not a gap glossed over.

          Like every other opt-in research module in this file, NOT wired
          into `rpc.py`, `scoring.WEIGHTS`, or `evidence.py`.
          Tests: `tests/test_population_rate.py` (14 cases: footprint/
          stratum validation, the closed-form interval against a hand-
          computed Gamma quantile and a `detected=0`/`exposure=0` sanity
          check, hierarchical-rate recovery of an injected `mu_log_rate`,
          `n_walkers` dimensionality validation, the anchor sweep's reuse
          of each anchor's own real `crossmatch.group_sources` count, and
          a static `rpc.py`-non-reference check) and
          `tests/test_population_rate_eval.py` (7 cases: synthetic-strata
          shape/validation, rate-posterior coverage reporting a plausible
          empirical rate, the anchor-bias study's naive-vs-corrected
          spread comparison and its input validation, and a static
          `rpc.py`-non-reference check). Full offline suite re-verified
          after this item landed: 2017 passed, 51 skipped, 0 failed.


[PARTIAL] Real-artifact hard-negative bank and domain adaptation (P0, roadmap item 33)  <-- NEW 2026-08-26
          `artifact_bank.py` (camera/CCD/night extraction, patch
          features, CORAL domain adaptation, classifier training) and
          `artifact_bank_eval.py` (cross-group AUPRC + the synthetic
          cross-survey arm, split purely for the 500-line guideline) are
          this item's module pair. A third, deliberately distinct sense
          of "artifact" in this codebase's vocabulary, named to avoid
          colliding with `artifact.py` (heuristic light-curve-feature
          verdict system) or `artifact_patches.py` (the real, TESS-only
          patch extractor this module builds directly on) -- both cross-
          referenced in its own module docstring.

          One real, already-documented constraint is stated up front, not
          worked around: ZTF has no real subtraction-artifact data this
          codebase can reach. `surveys/ztf.py` sends
          `BAD_CATFLAGS_MASK=32768` on every IRSA request, stripping
          bad-subtraction epochs server-side before ASTRA ever sees them,
          and `artifact.py` already investigated and rejected the one
          candidate real dataset (SNAD's ZTF DR3 artifact set) for the
          same reason. Recovering real ZTF artifacts means changing ZTF
          acquisition codebase-wide -- out of scope for one research
          module, the same restraint `artifact_patches.py`'s own
          docstring already states. This module is real for TESS and
          explicitly SYNTHETIC for the ZTF/cross-survey arm; every
          function/result touching that arm says so in a
          `"synthetic_proxy"` field, never presented as real ZTF
          performance.

          `artifact_patches.py`'s `extract_artifact_patches`/
          `CATEGORY_NAMES`/`categorize_quality` are reused UNCHANGED --
          `build_patch_bank` calls that function once PER TPF FILE
          (rather than once over a whole file list, its normal calling
          convention) specifically so real sector/camera/CCD/night
          metadata can be attached per patch, which the existing function
          has no way to carry. `night` is a stated, bounded approximation:
          one value per TPF FILE (from its first finite cadence
          timestamp), not a true per-patch timestamp, since recovering
          the latter would mean duplicating `extract_artifact_patches`'
          own windowing loop rather than reusing it.

          `extract_camera_ccd` reads `CAMERA`/`CCD` straight from a
          downloaded TPF's real primary FITS header via `astropy.io.fits`
          -- confirmed LIVE this session (not just assumed from
          documentation) against a real target (Pi Mensae, RA=84.2911,
          Dec=-80.4689, TESS sector 1): the real header keywords parsed to
          `CAMERA=4, CCD=2`, both physically valid TESS camera/CCD numbers
          (1-4). `night_bucket` converts the real, already-fully-
          referenced BJD `tess_pixels.read_tpf_cube`'s own `time` array
          carries (checked against `_time_to_bjd`'s real behaviour before
          writing this function, so it does NOT re-add the BTJD reference
          offset a naive reading of "TESS timestamps" might assume) to a
          UTC calendar-date string via `astropy.time.Time`.

          A second real, useful finding from the same live check: the
          real target's QUALITY column DOES carry real flagged cadences
          (10 "pointing", 5 "systematic" out of 1282 real cadences) but
          EVERY one is an isolated single-cadence run, below
          `extract_artifact_patches`'s default `min_run_length=3` --
          confirmed by inspecting the real run-length-encoded category
          sequence directly. This is real, pre-existing, correct behavior
          of the unmodified upstream function (a lone flagged cadence
          genuinely is not a stable "run" to build a patch around), not a
          bug this session introduced or fixed; `build_patch_bank`'s own
          live smoke test (`tests/test_artifact_bank_live.py`) uses
          `min_run_length=1` specifically to exercise the metadata-
          attachment path against this real file, documented in the test
          itself as a smoke-test-only override, not a changed default.

          **Domain adaptation** is CORAL (CORrelation ALignment; Sun &
          Saenko 2016, "Return of Frustratingly Easy Domain Adaptation,"
          AAAI) -- closed-form, needs no new dependency
          (`numpy.linalg.eigh` on the feature covariance, since a
          covariance matrix is symmetric positive semi-definite).
          Verified this session on a constructed covariate-shift toy set
          (two synthetic "cameras" sharing the same classification signal
          but offset by a large additive feature bias): `coral_align`'s
          output covariance moves measurably closer to the target
          domain's real covariance than the raw source's did, and
          `artifact_bank_eval.evaluate_cross_group_auprc`'s with-CORAL
          mean AUPRC is `&gt;=` its without-CORAL mean AUPRC under that same
          induced shift -- a real, checked numerical property, not merely
          asserted.

          **Features**: a small, bounded, hand-rolled 6-statistic vector
          (mean, std, MAD, max absolute deviation, a standardized third
          moment, longest run of masked-out points) fed into
          `sklearn.linear_model.LogisticRegression` -- matching this
          codebase's established "simple features into `LogisticRegression`"
          house style (`multimodal_eval.linear_probe_macro_f1`,
          `sn_classification_eval._macro_f1`, `significance.
          fit_selection_model`), not a new torch deep-net architecture.

          `artifact_bank_eval.evaluate_cross_group_auprc` is a real
          leave-one-group-out study over real TESS `PatchRecord`s, grouped
          by camera, CCD, or night. `evaluate_cross_survey_generalization_
          synthetic` is the honestly-labelled synthetic "unseen survey"
          arm: trains on real TESS features only, evaluates on real ZTF
          light-curve windows (`open_world_injection.extract_real_patches`,
          reused unchanged) with roughly half synthetically corrupted via
          `evaluate.py`'s existing `inject`/`ANOMALY_KINDS` (reused
          unchanged, no new corruption scheme invented) standing in for
          real ZTF artifacts that cannot be acquired. Returns
          `"n_ztf_windows_found": 0`/`"auprc": None` rather than a
          fabricated number when no real ZTF light curves exist in the
          local store, verified directly in tests.

          Like every other opt-in research module in this file, NOT wired
          into `rpc.py`, `scoring.WEIGHTS`, or `evidence.py`.
          Tests: `tests/test_artifact_bank.py` (15 cases: camera/CCD
          header parsing on constructed FITS files, night-bucket
          conversion, patch-feature shape/degenerate-input handling,
          CORAL's covariance-alignment property and input validation,
          classifier training/rejection, and a static `rpc.py`-non-
          reference check), `tests/test_artifact_bank_eval.py` (9 cases:
          grouped-split correctness, the CORAL-improves-held-out-AUPRC
          check under a real induced covariate shift, the synthetic
          cross-survey arm's valid-AUPRC and no-local-data paths using the
          real `isolated_root`/`store.write_curve` fixtures, and a static
          `rpc.py`-non-reference check), and `tests/test_artifact_bank_
          live.py` (1 case, live-marked, run and passed this session
          against a real downloaded TPF). Full offline suite re-verified
          after this item landed: 2041 passed, 52 skipped, 0 failed.


[PARTIAL] Conformal anomaly uncertainty (P1, roadmap item 34)  <-- NEW 2026-08-26
          `conformal.py` (calibration split, p-values, selective
          prediction, selective-risk curve) and `conformal_eval.py`
          (empirical coverage + the selective-risk-curve demonstration,
          split purely for the 500-line guideline) are this item's module
          pair. Unusual among this session's items: the core ARITHMETIC
          this item needs already existed, just not framed or validated
          as conformal. `significance.calibrate()`/`annotate()` compute,
          per score, `p = (1 + count(reference &gt;= score)) / (len(reference)
          + 1)` -- confirmed, by direct comparison, to be EXACTLY the
          standard split-conformal p-value formula (Vovk, Gammerman &
          Shafer 2005, *Algorithmic Learning in a Random World*, Ch. 2).
          `conformal_p_values` does not reimplement this -- it calls
          `significance.calibrate`/`annotate` directly, reused unchanged.
          Confirmed genuinely missing by exhaustive grep (zero hits for
          "conformal"/"selective_risk"/"risk_coverage"/"abstain" anywhere
          in `engine/astra` before this session): an enforced calibration/
          test split under a stated exchangeability assumption, empirical
          coverage validation, and any selective-risk/risk-coverage curve
          at all.

          Framing: conformal ANOMALY detection (Laxhammar & Falkman 2010,
          "Conformal prediction for distribution-independent anomaly
          detection") treats any existing anomaly score (`anomaly.py`'s
          per-detector scores or ensemble consensus, reused unchanged via
          plain `np.ndarray`s -- this module does not import `anomaly.py`
          itself, staying detector-agnostic) as a nonconformity measure
          against a held-out "normal" calibration population.
          `split_calibration_stream` states the exchangeability assumption
          explicitly (calibration and test points must be exchangeable
          draws from the same distribution under the null "this point is
          normal") rather than leaving it implicit the way `significance.
          calibrate` does.

          `selective_predict`/`selective_risk_coverage_curve` are
          genuinely new: per-point confidence is `2 * |p - 0.5|`,
          deliberately the COMPLEMENT of `review.select_next`'s own
          `tail_uncertainty` transform (`1.0 - abs(tail - 0.5) * 2.0`,
          `review.py` line ~60) -- reused for continuity with an existing
          in-house convention, not reinvented independently. At a target
          coverage level, the highest-confidence fraction is accepted
          (predicted "anomaly" when `p &lt; 0.5`, else "normal") and the rest
          abstain; risk is the misclassification rate among accepted
          decisions. Verified this session on a constructed separable
          synthetic case: risk is non-increasing as coverage shrinks to
          the most-confident points (`test_selective_risk_coverage_curve_
          risk_is_non_increasing_as_coverage_shrinks`), a real, checked
          monotonicity property of selective prediction, not merely
          "produces a curve."

          `conformal_eval.evaluate_conformal_coverage_synthetic` verifies
          the exact theoretical guarantee split-conformal prediction
          makes -- for calibration/test scores drawn i.i.d. from the
          IDENTICAL distribution (exchangeable by construction), the
          empirical false-positive rate at flagging threshold `alpha`
          matches nominal `alpha` within a Wilson 95% CI
          (`significance._ci_binomial`, reused unchanged) -- verified
          directly this session over 150 trials at 3 alpha levels, not
          merely cited as a property. A second, independent uniformity
          check (`test_conformal_p_values_are_uniform_for_exchangeable_
          normal_draws`) runs a Kolmogorov-Smirnov test against
          Uniform(0,1) on 500 real conformal p-values and does not reject.

          `conformal_eval.evaluate_conformal_coverage_on_injected_
          anomalies` is the one real integration-level check in this
          pair: reuses `evaluate.py`'s real injection-recovery harness
          (`build_injected`/`sequence_summary`) and `anomaly.detect()`'s
          real ensemble consensus score (both reused UNCHANGED) rather
          than only the abstract synthetic case, grounding the coverage
          guarantee in this codebase's actual anomaly pipeline. Real
          deployment data is not, in general, provably exchangeable with
          a held-out calibration set -- stated explicitly as a real
          limitation of this check, not a substitute for that assumption.

          Explicitly NOT done, matching this session's established
          convention: does not touch or duplicate `significance.
          calibrate`'s existing `rpc.py` exposure (`"significance.
          calibrate"`) -- calls the same function, does not fork it; does
          not add a field to `candidates.Candidate`; does not touch
          `review.py`'s scoring formula (confirmed `review.select_next`
          already consumes a calibrated `significance.tail_probability`
          with a fixed 0.20 weight -- wiring a conformal p-value into that
          formula is a real, separate decision, deliberately left
          unmade). Like every other opt-in research module in this file,
          NOT wired into `rpc.py`, `scoring.WEIGHTS`, or `evidence.py`.

          Tests: `tests/test_conformal.py` (13 cases: calibration-split
          partition/validation, p-value arithmetic against a hand-computed
          value and the KS-uniformity check, alpha-flagging, selective-
          prediction fraction/ordering correctness, the risk-coverage
          monotonicity check, and a static `rpc.py`-non-reference check)
          and `tests/test_conformal_eval.py` (7 cases: synthetic coverage
          matching nominal alpha within CI, the real `anomaly.detect()`
          integration study running end-to-end, the selective-risk-curve
          demonstration and its input validation, and a static `rpc.py`-
          non-reference check). Full offline suite re-verified after this
          item landed: 2061 passed, 52 skipped, 0 failed.


[PARTIAL] Causal CCD-artifact attribution (P2, exploratory, roadmap item 35)  <-- NEW 2026-08-26
          `ccd_attribution.py` (background-level extraction, covariate
          design matrix, the covariate-adjustment model, `adjusted_
          scores`) and `ccd_attribution_eval.py` (the leave-one-camera-
          out false-positive-reduction study + a controlled synthetic
          demonstration, split purely for the 500-line guideline) are
          this item's module pair. The lowest-priority, most exploratory
          item in this backlog, and scoped accordingly: a read-only
          feasibility scan BEFORE writing any plan (not just before
          writing code) confirmed this item's own data list overclaims
          what this codebase can actually reach.

          Confirmed UNREACHABLE, stated up front rather than worked
          around: real DETECTOR TELEMETRY (CCD temperature/voltage/gain,
          gyro/attitude housekeeping) -- `tess_pixels.py`'s TPF reader
          parses only `TIME`/`FLUX`/`FLUX_ERR`/`QUALITY`/`BJDREFI`/
          `BJDREFF`/`TIMEUNIT`/`TIMESYS` from the FITS header, confirmed
          by reading it; true engineering telemetry is a separate MAST
          product this codebase does not download. Real "OBSERVING
          CONDITIONS" (seeing, airmass, moon illumination): zero hits
          anywhere in `engine/astra` for either survey. Real CAUSAL-
          INFERENCE machinery (SCM, do-calculus, propensity scores): zero
          hits anywhere in `engine/astra`, and no randomized/natural-
          experiment structure exists in real TESS data to identify one
          from. None of this is silently worked around -- `ccd_
          attribution.py`'s own module docstring states all three gaps
          explicitly before describing what it actually does.

          What IS real and reachable: `artifact_bank.py`'s (item #33)
          real `camera`/`ccd`/`night` per real TESS patch, reused
          UNCHANGED (`PatchRecord`, `build_patch_bank`, `patch_features`,
          `train_hard_negative_classifier`), plus one new real covariate
          this item adds: `extract_background_level`, the median across
          real cadences of each cadence's real whole-frame background
          (the same quantity `artifact_patches._cadence_flux` already
          computes internally and discards, exposed here as its own
          value) -- one value per TPF FILE, not a true per-patch value,
          the identical approximation `artifact_bank.py`'s own `night`
          field already uses and documents, applied consistently rather
          than inventing a different convention.

          Given no causal-graph/do-calculus/randomized structure is
          available, this module does NOT claim genuine causal
          identification. It implements the standard, citable
          alternative: REGRESSION/COVARIATE ADJUSTMENT (Rubin 1974's
          potential-outcomes framing of a "counterfactual" outcome under
          a different covariate assignment; Pearl 2009, *Causality*, 2nd
          ed., the "backdoor adjustment"/g-computation formula in its
          simplest linear form) -- `fit_covariate_adjustment` fits
          `is_artifact ~ camera + ccd + background` via `sklearn.
          LogisticRegression`; `counterfactual_probability` evaluates
          that model at an arbitrary (possibly counterfactual) covariate
          assignment; `adjusted_scores` subtracts the covariate-
          attributable portion of a raw classifier's score (evaluated at
          the patch's real covariates minus at a reference covariate
          assignment) from that score, a standard "partial out a
          covariate" construction. Verified this session:
          `test_fit_covariate_adjustment_recovers_a_known_camera_
          prevalence_effect` confirms the model recovers a known,
          injected camera-prevalence effect from synthetic covariate-
          label data, and `test_adjusted_scores_leaves_the_reference_
          camera_unchanged` confirms the arithmetic is exactly zero-effect
          at the reference covariate assignment, by construction.

          `ccd_attribution_eval.evaluate_counterfactual_removal_synthetic`
          targets a specific, real, well-documented statistical
          phenomenon (not an invented one): when a classifier is trained
          on data POOLED across groups with genuinely different true
          prevalence and OVERLAPPING (not perfectly separable) feature
          distributions, the pooled decision threshold is systematically
          miscalibrated per group relative to each group's own base rate
          -- the same reasoning behind prior/base-rate correction in
          imbalanced classification (e.g. Elkan 2001, "The Foundations of
          Cost-Sensitive Learning"). A real, honest correction was made
          to this function's OWN default parameters this session: the
          first synthetic construction tried (well-separated classes,
          `signal_gap=1.5`) produced a trivial 0.0/0.0 false-positive-rate
          tie for both cameras -- checked directly by printing the actual
          result, not assumed passing from a green test alone -- which
          would have made the test vacuously true. Tuned to a genuinely
          overlapping regime (`signal_gap=1.0`, `overlap_scale=1.5`) and
          re-verified across 10 independent seeds before relying on it:
          the non-reference camera's own false-positive rate is real and
          nonzero (0.014-0.042 across seeds) and adjustment reduces it in
          every seed tried, a mechanistically guaranteed property of the
          construction (camera 2's learned covariate effect is positive
          since its true prevalence exceeds the reference's, so
          subtracting it from camera 2's raw scores can only ever lower
          them). The WEAKER, non-robust claim tried first -- that
          adjustment converges the cross-camera false-positive-rate
          SPREAD to parity -- was found, by direct inspection across a
          parameter sweep, to sometimes overshoot past the reference
          camera's own rate rather than landing exactly at it; the final
          test checks the robust per-camera-2 reduction, not the
          non-robust spread-convergence claim, and the module's own
          docstring states this distinction explicitly.

          `ccd_attribution_eval.evaluate_counterfactual_false_positive_
          reduction` is the real leave-one-camera-out study (same shape
          as `artifact_bank_eval.evaluate_cross_group_auprc`), comparing
          RAW vs. ADJUSTED AUPRC/false-positive rate on real TESS
          `CovariatePatch`es. No synthetic ZTF arm this time, unlike item
          #33 -- this item's own data list is pixel-cutout-centric and
          does not name ZTF.

          Like every other opt-in research module in this file, NOT wired
          into `rpc.py`, `scoring.WEIGHTS`, or `evidence.py`.
          Tests: `tests/test_ccd_attribution.py` (10 cases: background
          extraction on a missing file, design-matrix shape/imputation/
          validation, the known-camera-effect recovery check, the
          reference-covariate zero-effect arithmetic check, and a static
          `rpc.py`-non-reference check) and `tests/test_ccd_attribution_
          eval.py` (10 cases, including the camera-2-false-positive-
          reduction check parametrized across 5 seeds, the leave-one-
          camera-out study running end-to-end, and a static `rpc.py`-
          non-reference check). Full offline suite re-verified after this
          item landed: 2083 passed, 52 skipped, 0 failed.


[PARTIAL] Active-learning researcher review (P1, roadmap item 36)  <-- NEW 2026-08-26
          `active_review.py` (label history, per-reason yield, learned
          reason weights, reweighted selection, the reviewer-agreement
          proxy) and `active_review_eval.py` (the reweighting
          integration study + the synthetic dual-reviewer-kappa
          mechanism check, split purely for the 500-line guideline) are
          this item's module pair. `review.py` and `candidates.py` were
          read in full before writing this item: `review.select_next`
          is a transparent, FIXED-weight heuristic (0.45 disagreement +
          0.35 artifact uncertainty + 0.20 tail uncertainty, `review.py`
          line ~63) that never updates from accumulated labels -- this
          item adds exactly that closed loop, calling `select_next` and
          `candidates.load_labels`/`label_summary` UNCHANGED rather than
          re-deriving uncertainty or diversity scoring.

          Framing: learning which of several existing acquisition
          signals is most informative from observed labels is a real,
          named active-learning strategy -- online/bandit selection
          AMONG acquisition functions (Baram, El-Yaniv & Luz 2004,
          "Online Choice of Active Learning Algorithms," JMLR 5).
          `reason_yield`/`learn_reason_weights` narrow this to
          `select_next`'s own four fixed, human-readable `reasons` tags
