          ("detectors disagree", "artifact assessment is uncertain",
          "significance is near the review boundary", "diverse
          candidate") as the arms: each candidate's OWN reasons (public
          output, never `select_next`'s internal formula) are matched
          against real human labels to compute a Laplace-smoothed
          POSITIVE rate per reason. `reweighted_select_next` then re-
          scores `select_next`'s full-pool ranking by the mean learned
          weight of each candidate's reasons and excludes already-
          labelled candidates -- an active-review queue should surface
          unlabelled work. Verified this session on a constructed
          synthetic pool: a deliberately higher-yield reason is both
          assigned a higher learned weight AND enriched among the top
          selections on a FRESH, disjoint unlabelled pool from the same
          distribution (`evaluate_reweighted_selection_recovers_high_
          yield_reason_synthetic`), a real, checkable property of the
          reweighting mechanism, not merely "weights get produced."

          Confirmed REACHABLE for "labels required per recovered rare-
          object": `metadata.labels` (`metadata.py` line ~40) stores a
          real `recorded_utc` per label, so a genuine chronological
          label history exists in this codebase. `labels_per_recovery`
          walks that real history and counts labels between successive
          POSITIVE ("interesting"/"needs_follow_up") labels directly --
          not a synthetic proxy.

          Confirmed UNREACHABLE, stated up front rather than worked
          around: TRUE inter-rater reviewer agreement. `metadata.
          labels`' schema keys the `labels` table on `candidate_key`
          ALONE (`metadata.py` line ~40-43, PRIMARY KEY) -- one label
          per candidate, overwritten on relabel; there is nowhere a
          second, independent reviewer's label could even be stored.
          `reviewer_agreement_with_priority` is therefore a stated
          PROXY -- agreement between a human's final label and whether
          `select_next`'s own priority crossed a threshold -- gated the
          same way `review.evaluate` gates (`MIN_LABELS`/
          `MIN_PER_CLASS`, reused unchanged), using real Cohen's kappa
          (`sklearn.metrics.cohen_kappa_score`, already a dependency via
          `review.evaluate`) against real labels. Since real dual-
          reviewer data does not exist to check the KAPPA MECHANISM
          itself against, `evaluate_synthetic_dual_reviewer_kappa`
          exercises it on synthetic two-reviewer labels at controlled
          true-agreement levels and confirms kappa is non-decreasing in
          the injected agreement level -- verified this session across
          4 agreement levels, with kappa == 1.0 exactly at perfect
          injected agreement.

          Explicitly NOT done: does not modify `review.py` or
          `candidates.py` in any way -- `select_next`, `record_label`,
          and `load_labels` are called, never forked. Does not add a
          field to `candidates.Candidate`. Like every other opt-in
          research module in this codebase, NOT wired into `rpc.py`,
          `scoring.WEIGHTS`, or `evidence.py`.

          Tests: `tests/test_active_review.py` (14 cases: label-history
          ordering, recovery-gap counting, reason-yield/weight-learning
          correctness including the cold-start uniform-weight default,
          reweighted-selection exclusion of labelled candidates and
          upweighted-reason bias, the reviewer-agreement gate, and a
          static `rpc.py`-non-reference check) and `tests/test_active_
          review_eval.py` (6 cases, including the reweighting
          integration study and the dual-reviewer-kappa monotonicity
          check, and a static `rpc.py`-non-reference check). Full
          offline suite re-verified after this item landed: 2103
          passed, 52 skipped, 0 failed.
          UPDATE 2026-08-28: `active_review.py` (the reweighting logic
          itself, NOT `active_review_eval.py`, which stays research-only)
          deliberately promoted into the live `review.next` RPC path,
          gated behind a new `active` flag (default `false`, byte-for-
          byte unchanged `review.select_next` behaviour). `rpc.py:
          _handle_review_next` calls `active_review.learn_reason_weights`
          + `reweighted_select_next` when `active=True`; threaded through
          `src-tauri/src/lib.rs:engine_review_next` (new `Option<bool>`
          param, same pattern as the existing `fit_model` param) and
          `src/lib/engine.ts:reviewNext` up to a new "Active learning"
          toggle in `ExplainPanel.tsx` next to the existing "Review next"
          button. The two static non-reference guard tests were updated
          rather than deleted: `test_active_review_is_not_wired_into_rpc`
          -> `test_active_review_is_wired_into_rpc_for_active_mode`
          (asserts the module IS now referenced), and `test_active_
          review_eval.py`'s guard narrowed to check `active_review_eval`
          specifically (still absent from rpc.py) rather than the
          `active_review` substring it used to match. New RPC-level test:
          `tests/test_rpc.py::test_review_next_active_mode_excludes_
          labelled_candidates`. This promotion does not change any of the
          caveats documented above -- the reviewer-agreement metric is
          still a stated proxy, reason weights still cold-start uniform,
          and the reason vocabulary is still the same fixed four tags --
          it only makes the already-tested reweighting reachable from the
          live UI. No change to `review.py`, `candidates.py`, `active_
          review.py` itself, or `metadata.py`'s label schema.


[PARTIAL] Information-gain follow-up scheduler (P1, roadmap item 37)  <-- NEW 2026-08-26
          `information_gain.py` (binary posterior entropy, `rank_by_
          information_gain`) and `information_gain_eval.py` (the
          entropy-maximum sanity check, the synthetic feasibility-
          ranking study, and one real `followup.plan` integration
          check, split purely for the 500-line guideline) are this
          item's module pair. `followup.py` was read in full before
          writing this item: `followup.plan` already computes real
          airmass/twilight/lunar-separation/weather/facility geometry
          for ONE target (`plan(*, ra_deg, dec_deg, ...)`, `followup.py`
          line ~134) but never ranks or compares multiple candidates --
          confirmed genuinely missing by grep for "information_gain"/
          "entropy"/"expected_value" (zero hits) before this session.
          This item calls `followup.plan` UNCHANGED once per candidate
          for the visibility/feasibility layer rather than re-deriving
          any sidereal-time, solar, or lunar geometry.

          Framing: Bayesian experimental design values a proposed
          observation by the expected REDUCTION in posterior entropy it
          would produce (Lindley 1956, "On a Measure of the Information
          Provided by an Experiment," Annals of Mathematical
          Statistics). `posterior_entropy` is the standard binary
          Shannon entropy of a candidate's "is this real?" posterior;
          `entropy_from_tail_probability` accepts either `significance.
          calibrate`/`annotate`'s tail probability or `conformal.
          conformal_p_values`'s p-value UNCHANGED, since item 34's own
          docstring already establishes these are the same empirical-
          tail quantity. Confirmed UNREACHABLE, stated up front: a real
          per-instrument measurement-noise model for what a follow-up
          observation would actually resolve -- no survey connector
          records a per-exposure depth/SNR model this codebase could use
          for an exact myopic expected-posterior update. `rank_by_
          information_gain` therefore uses the standard, citable
          IDEALIZED upper bound the active-learning literature uses when
          no noise model is available: a noiseless follow-up is assumed
          to fully resolve current uncertainty, so expected gain is
          approximated by the CURRENT entropy itself (Houlsby, Huszar,
          Lengyel & Ghahramani 2011, "Bayesian Active Learning for
          Classification and Preference Learning," uses the same
          current-entropy term as the dominant component of their BALD
          acquisition function). Stated explicitly as an upper bound,
          not an exact expected gain, in the module's own docstring.

          `rank_by_information_gain` divides that entropy by
          `exposure_hours` (the assumed cost of one follow-up
          observation) for the roadmap item's own "expected posterior
          entropy reduction per observing hour" metric, and ranks any
          candidate `followup.plan` itself reports as not visible last
          with `value_per_hour=None`, reusing its real `visible`/
          `windows` output rather than reimplementing feasibility.
          Verified this session: a real (non-synthetic) integration
          check confirms a target at dec=-85 (from the default site,
          latitude 43.65) that `followup.plan` itself reports as never
          visible under permissive constraints is reported infeasible
          by `rank_by_information_gain` via the same real call
          (`evaluate_real_followup_infeasibility_integration`), and a
          synthetic study confirms no infeasible candidate ever outranks
          a feasible one, with feasible candidates ordered by distance
          of their posterior from 0.5 as entropy requires.

          Explicitly NOT done: does not modify `followup.py` in any way
          -- `plan` is called once per candidate, never forked. Does not
          import `anomaly.py` or `conformal.py`; takes plain posterior-
          probability floats, staying detector-agnostic like
          `conformal.py`. Like every other opt-in research module in
          this codebase, NOT wired into `rpc.py`, `scoring.WEIGHTS`, or
          `evidence.py`.

          Tests: `tests/test_information_gain.py` (9 cases: entropy
          boundary/maximum values, input validation, ranking order by
          feasibility then entropy-per-hour, exposure-hours scaling, and
          a static `rpc.py`-non-reference check) and `tests/test_
          information_gain_eval.py` (4 cases, including the real
          `followup.plan` infeasibility-integration check, and a static
          `rpc.py`-non-reference check). Full offline suite re-verified
          after this item landed: 2120 passed, 52 skipped, 0 failed.


[DONE]    Follow-up request/result tracking  <-- NEW 2026-08-28
          `followup.plan` (`followup.py`) was, and remains, a pure stateless
          visibility/scheduling calculation for ONE target -- it never
          recorded that a request was actually made, ending every UI call
          with "No observation request was submitted" (`ExplainPanel.tsx`).
          This item adds the tracking layer on top, without touching
          `followup.py` itself (same "does not modify `followup.py`"
          discipline the information-gain item above states): a new
          `followup_requests` table in `metadata.py` (SCHEMA_VERSION 6->7),
          modelled on `event_packets`' one-candidate-to-many-rows,
          append-only shape rather than `labels`' single-row-per-candidate
          shape, since a candidate can legitimately be requested for
          follow-up more than once over its lifetime. New functions:
          `metadata.put_followup_request`/`put_followup_result`/
          `followup_history`, wrapped by `candidates.request_followup`/
          `record_followup_result`/`followup_history` (request IDs are
          `uuid4`-based, matching `jobs.py`'s `job_id` pattern -- not
          content-hashed, since these are user-initiated actions, not
          idempotent-retry-of-external-feed packets). New RPC methods
          `followup.request`/`followup.result`/`followup.history`
          (`rpc.py`, beside the existing `followup.plan`), threaded through
          new Tauri commands (`engine_followup_request/_result/_history`,
          `src-tauri/src/lib.rs`) and typed `engine.ts` wrappers
          (`FollowupRequestEntry`). UI: `ExplainPanel.tsx`'s `FollowupDraft`
          gained a "Follow-up tracking" section below the existing
          visibility-planning UI -- request a follow-up (facility + note),
          load/refresh history, and record an outcome (observed/no_show/
          cancelled) per open request. Result statuses are a distinct,
          smaller set (`FOLLOWUP_RESULT_STATUSES`) from the candidate
          `LABELS` vocabulary -- a follow-up outcome is not a researcher
          judgement label. Tests: `tests/test_candidates.py::
          TestFollowupTracking` (6 cases: create/read round trip, multiple
          requests per candidate, result recording, unknown-status
          rejection, result-for-unknown-request KeyError, empty history)
          and `tests/test_rpc.py::test_followup_request_result_and_
          history_round_trip`. Full offline suite re-verified after this
          item landed: 2302 passed, 52 skipped, 0 failed. `cargo check`
          and `tsc --noEmit` both clean.


[PARTIAL] Detector/survey quality drift monitor (P0, roadmap item 38)  <-- NEW 2026-08-26
          `quality_drift.py` (z-score standardization, two-sided CUSUM,
          change-point detection with reset, bootstrap threshold
          calibration, multi-channel monitoring) and `quality_drift_
          eval.py` (the calibration-achieves-target-FPR study and the
          detection-delay/false-alarm-rate study -- the roadmap item's
          own two named metrics -- split purely for the 500-line
          guideline) are this item's module pair. Confirmed fully
          greenfield by exhaustive grep for "zero_point"/"zeropoint"/
          "seeing"/"sky_brightness"/"calibrat"/"drift" across `sweep.
          py`/`surveys/__init__.py`/`netclient.py` before this session:
          zero hits. `agn_changepoint.py` (items ~16-20) was read in
          full and explicitly NOT reused: it fits a `celerite2` damped-
          random-walk GP to ASTROPHYSICAL light curves, a different
          statistical model for a different kind of signal -- a nightly
          calibration-metric stream has no reason to follow a DRW
          process, so reusing that machinery would be a category error,
          not code reuse.

          Framing: Page's (1954) CUSUM control chart ("Continuous
          Inspection Schemes," Biometrika 41) is the standard, purpose-
          built tool for detecting a persistent mean shift in a
          monitored stream while controlling false alarms; design and
          calibration follow Basseville & Nikiforov (1993, *Detection of
          Abrupt Changes*, Ch. 2). This module is deliberately CHANNEL-
          AGNOSTIC like `conformal.py`: any 1-D array (zero point,
          seeing, sky brightness, or alert rate) is just "a monitored
          stream" to it; `monitor_multiple_channels` is a thin per-
          channel convenience loop, not survey-specific integration.

          A real, honest correction was made to `calibrate_threshold`
          this session: the first version bootstrap-resampled the
          nominal reference WITH REPLACEMENT (an ordinary nonparametric
          bootstrap) to calibrate the alarm threshold. Checked directly
          rather than assumed correct, this UNDER-estimated the true
          max-CUSUM distribution's tail (90th percentile 6.57 vs. 7.82
          on a matched trial) -- a known failure mode of the ordinary
          bootstrap for EXTREME-VALUE statistics, since resampling can
          never exceed the finite reference sample's own realized
          extremes. Replaced with a PARAMETRIC bootstrap under a
          Gaussian null (fresh synthetic draws from `Normal(reference_
          mean, reference_std)`), mirroring `agn_changepoint.calibrate_
          changepoint_significance`'s own choice to sample from the
          FITTED model rather than resample the observed curve. A
          SECOND real effect was found and documented rather than
          patched over: with a short (200-point) reference, plug-in
          estimation noise in `reference_mean`/`reference_std` still
          left the empirical false-positive rate at 38% against a 10%
          target on genuinely fresh out-of-sample data; lengthening the
          reference to 1000 points dropped it to 12.6%, confirming the
          cause and now stated as a real, load-bearing caveat in
          `calibrate_threshold`'s own docstring -- callers need a long
          drift-free reference, not just a calibration procedure that is
          correct in the large-reference limit.

          `quality_drift_eval.evaluate_detection_delay_and_false_alarm_
          rate_synthetic` computes the roadmap item's own two named
          metrics directly: mean/median samples-to-first-alarm after a
          known injected shift, and the false-alarm rate on pure-
          nominal streams, both with a Wilson 95% CI (`significance.
          _ci_binomial`, reused unchanged). Real live nightly telemetry
          does not exist in this codebase (confirmed by the grep above),
          so both studies use synthetic streams, honestly labelled.

          Explicitly NOT done: does not modify `agn_changepoint.py`,
          `sweep.py`, or any survey connector. Like every other opt-in
          research module in this codebase, NOT wired into `rpc.py`,
          `scoring.WEIGHTS`, or `evidence.py`.

          Tests: `tests/test_quality_drift.py` (14 cases: standardization
          arithmetic, CUSUM growth under a sustained shift, change-point
          detection/reset behaviour, bootstrap-threshold determinism and
          input validation, multi-channel monitoring, and a static
          `rpc.py`-non-reference check) and `tests/test_quality_drift_
          eval.py` (5 cases, including the calibration-achieves-target-
          FPR study and the detection-delay/false-alarm-rate study, and
          a static `rpc.py`-non-reference check). Full offline suite
          re-verified after this item landed: 2139 passed, 52 skipped,
          0 failed.


[PARTIAL] Federated multi-observatory training (P2, roadmap item 39)  <-- NEW 2026-08-26
          `federated_training.py` (institution partitioning, additive-
          masking secure aggregation, FedAvg parameter averaging, one
          federated round with optional CORAL domain alignment) and
          `federated_training_eval.py` (the secure-aggregation exact-sum/
          leakage check + the federated-vs-centralized accuracy-gap
          study, split purely for the 500-line guideline) are this
          item's module pair. Confirmed fully greenfield by grep for
          "distributed"/"multi.node"/"federat"/"torch.distributed"/
          "DataParallel"/"horovod" across `tensors.py`/`train.py`/
          `models.py` before this session: zero hits.

          Scoped realistically to what is checkable OFFLINE, stated up
          front: no real multi-institution network, auth, or transport
          layer exists anywhere in this codebase (confirmed absent) --
          this module simulates the AGGREGATION MATHEMATICS in one
          process, not a deployment or network-security demonstration.
          The classifier is `sklearn.linear_model.LogisticRegression`
          (`artifact_bank.train_hard_negative_classifier`, reused
          UNCHANGED per institution), matching this codebase's house
          "simple features into LogisticRegression" style and, more
          importantly, making "average the parameters" (FedAvg; McMahan,
          Moore, Ramage, Hampson & y Arcas 2017, "Communication-
          Efficient Learning of Deep Networks from Decentralized Data,"
          AISTATS) a well-defined, exact operation -- not true in
          general for a deep net's non-convex loss. `_set_linear_
          params` constructs an averaged estimator by assigning `coef_`/
          `intercept_`/`classes_` directly, confirmed by direct API
          inspection this session that `LogisticRegression.predict`
          reads only those three fitted attributes.

          **Secure aggregation** implements the CORE additive-masking
          idea of Bonawitz et al. (2017, "Practical Secure Aggregation
          for Federated Learning," CCS): for every client pair, a shared
          random mask is added to one and subtracted from the other, so
          `sum(masks) == 0` exactly and the true sum is recoverable
          without any individual client update being exposed in the
          clear. Bonawitz et al.'s cryptographic key-agreement/dropout-
          recovery layers are explicitly NOT implemented -- there is no
          real network transport in this codebase for them to protect,
          so building that machinery would have nothing real to secure.
          Verified this session (`evaluate_secure_aggregation_recovers_
          exact_sum`): the aggregate matches the true elementwise sum to
          floating-point precision, masks sum to exactly zero, and no
          individual masked vector equals its true unmasked counterpart
          -- the roadmap item's own "leakage audit," run against the
          real masking arithmetic.

          **Domain adaptation** reuses `artifact_bank.coral_align`
          UNCHANGED (CORAL; Sun & Saenko 2016). A real, honest
          correction was made to `federated_round`'s alignment target
          this session: the first version aligned every institution's
          features to the FIRST shard's domain (arbitrary), which
          measurably made federated accuracy WORSE than no alignment at
          all (0.67 vs. 0.82 on a synthetic institution-shift study) --
          checked directly by printing the actual accuracies, not
          assumed. Root cause: `artifact_bank_eval.evaluate_cross_group_
          auprc` already establishes this codebase's own CORAL
          convention -- align TRAIN features to the DEPLOYMENT/test
          domain, not an arbitrary other group, "so the SAME classifier
          consumes already-aligned features, matching CORAL's intended
          use." `federated_round` now takes an explicit `reference_
          features` parameter so a caller can follow that same
          convention; re-verified after the fix, alignment narrows the
          accuracy gap (0.058 aligned vs. 0.075 unaligned against a
          0.892 centralized baseline) rather than widening it.

          Confirmed UNREACHABLE, stated up front: real cross-institution
          deployment concerns (authentication, network partitioning,
          differential-privacy budgeting, dropout/failure recovery
          during a real multi-party protocol) -- nothing real in this
          codebase to integrate with on that side.

          Explicitly NOT done: does not modify `artifact_bank.py` --
          `coral_align` and `train_hard_negative_classifier` are called
          unchanged. Like every other opt-in research module in this
          codebase, NOT wired into `rpc.py`, `scoring.WEIGHTS`, or
          `evidence.py`.

          Tests: `tests/test_federated_training.py` (10 cases:
          partitioning coverage, pairwise-mask zero-sum, secure-
          aggregate exact-sum correctness and input validation, FedAvg
          matching a plain mean for identical models, a full federated
          round producing a usable classifier, and a static `rpc.py`-
          non-reference check) and `tests/test_federated_training_
          eval.py` (5 cases, including the exact-sum/leakage check and
          the accuracy-gap study with alignment narrowing the gap, and a
          static `rpc.py`-non-reference check). Full offline suite re-
          verified after this item landed: 2154 passed, 52 skipped,
          0 failed.


[PARTIAL] Signed reproducibility bundles (P0, roadmap item 40)  <-- NEW 2026-08-26
          `reproducibility_bundle.py` (Ed25519 keypair generation, the
          bundle concept, signing/verification, bitwise and tolerance-
          bounded rerun checks) and `reproducibility_bundle_eval.py`
          (the signed round-trip + tamper-detection study and the
          synthetic rerun study, split purely for the 500-line
          guideline) are this item's module pair. `manifest.py` was read
          in full before writing this item and is NOT reimplemented:
          `Manifest`/`SurveyQuery` already record what a query asked
          for, `compute_content_hash`/`seal`/`verify` already hash the
          query (not the timestamp) so identical requests hash
          identically, and `capture_environment` already records the
          software/hardware context -- all reused UNCHANGED. Confirmed
          genuinely missing by grep for "sign"/"signature"/"ed25519"/
          "public_key" (zero hits) across `engine/astra` before this
          session: cryptographic signing, and a BUNDLE concept packaging
          a manifest with environment and experiment-record references
          into one distributable, independently-verifiable unit.

          No pure-stdlib asymmetric-signature primitive exists in
          CPython -- confirmed by checking, not assumed -- so `cryptography`
          (PyCA) was added to `engine/pyproject.toml`'s `research` extra
          this session (`cryptography==50.0.1`, installed and verified
          working in this environment), matching this codebase's
          existing house pattern for an optional research dependency
          (`agn_changepoint.py`'s `celerite2`, `microlensing_fit.py`'s
          `emcee`): a lazy `_require_cryptography()` import guard raising
          `ReproducibilityBundleError` with an install instruction when
          absent, never a hard top-level import. Signing uses Ed25519
          (Bernstein, Duif, Lange, Schwabe & Yang 2012, "High-speed
          high-security signatures," J. Cryptographic Engineering).

          **Bundle vs. manifest, precisely, stated in the module's own
          docstring**: a bundle packages a sealed manifest's `content_
          hash` (query provenance) with `environment` and a list of
          experiment-record references into ONE payload with its OWN
          `bundle_hash` (`compute_bundle_hash`) -- separate from the
          manifest's own hash, since one manifest can be bundled into
          different experiment contexts. `verify_bundle` checks BOTH
          that the recorded `bundle_hash` still matches recomputing it
          from the bundle's own fields (TAMPER DETECTION on content) AND
          that the Ed25519 signature verifies against the recorded
          public key (PROVENANCE VERIFICATION), returning `False` --
          never raising -- on any failure. Verified this session
          (`evaluate_signed_bundle_round_trip`, run directly and its
          real output inspected, not merely asserted in a test): a
          validly signed bundle verifies; an unsigned bundle, and a
          bundle with its hash, environment, or experiment-record refs
          each independently mutated after signing, are all correctly
          rejected; verification also correctly fails under a
          substituted (wrong) public key.

          **Bitwise/tolerance-bounded rerun, precisely**: `manifest.
          Manifest.compute_content_hash` only ever hashed the QUERY, by
          the module's own original design ("a manifest is a few
          hundred kilobytes where a snapshot is tens of gigabytes") --
          it deliberately does not hash retrieved DATA. `verify_
          manifest_rerun` reuses that query-identity check UNCHANGED for
          the provenance half of "rerun." For the DATA half -- genuinely
          new, confirmed nothing in this codebase hashes or tolerance-
          compares result arrays today -- `hash_array_bitwise`/`verify_
          rerun_bitwise` do an exact byte-for-byte SHA-256 check, and
          `verify_rerun_tolerance` does a `numpy.allclose` check while
          reporting the actual maximum absolute/relative deviation, not
          only a boolean. Confirmed UNREACHABLE, stated up front: a real
          live re-fetch of survey data to check rerun equality against
          -- no network access exists in this offline session to re-
          query ZTF, Gaia, or TESS, so `reproducibility_bundle_eval.py`'s
          rerun study uses synthetic arrays standing in for a rerun,
          honestly labelled as such. Verified this session: the bitwise
          check accepts an identical array and rejects a changed one;
          the tolerance check accepts a perturbation at the 1e-10 scale
          under `rtol=1e-6`/`atol=1e-8` while correctly rejecting a
          perturbation of magnitude 1.0, reporting the real deviation
          (1.7e-10 vs. 1.0 max absolute difference across the two cases).

          Explicitly NOT done: does not modify `manifest.py` -- every
          function it exposes is called unchanged. Like every other
          opt-in research module in this codebase, NOT wired into
          `rpc.py`, `scoring.WEIGHTS`, or `evidence.py`.

          Tests: `tests/test_reproducibility_bundle.py` (13 cases:
          keypair determinism, unsealed-manifest rejection, the sign/
          verify round trip, tampered-hash/environment/wrong-public-key
          rejection, manifest-rerun match/mismatch, bitwise and
          tolerance rerun checks and their input validation, and a
          static `rpc.py`-non-reference check) and `tests/test_
          reproducibility_bundle_eval.py` (3 cases: the full signed
          round-trip study, the synthetic rerun study, and a static
          `rpc.py`-non-reference check) -- all `pytest.importorskip
          ("cryptography")`-gated, matching this codebase's existing
          convention for `research`-extra-gated tests. Full offline
          suite re-verified after this item landed: 2171 passed,
          52 skipped, 0 failed.

          All five items from this batch (36-40) are now landed. Grepped
          `rpc.py`, `scoring.py`, and `evidence.py` for every new
          module's name after all five: zero hits in every case,
          confirming none was accidentally wired in.


---------------------------------------------------------------------
ASTROPHYSICS & EXTRATERRESTRIAL-STUDY FEATURE PASS  <-- NEW 2026-08-31
---------------------------------------------------------------------

Five new capability verticals, each a full vertical slice (engine
module + paired `*_eval.py` + tests + `rpc.py` handler + Tauri command
+ `engine.ts` method + React panel + Sidebar/App wiring, under a new
"Frontier" sidebar group). All five follow this codebase's existing
"diagnostic evidence only" convention: grepped `scoring.py` and
`evidence.py` for every new module's name after all five landed --
zero hits in every case, confirming none was folded into ranking.
Every `*_eval.py` module is confirmed absent from `rpc.py` by its own
`test_not_referenced_by_rpc` test, the same convention every other
eval module in this codebase already follows.

Literature coefficients transcribed for this pass were fetched and
read from the primary source this session where the source was openly
accessible (Kopparapu et al. 2013 ApJ 765,131 AND its erratum ApJ
770,82 -- both PDFs fetched directly, Table 3's ERRATUM values used,
not the superseded originally-published ones; Bowell et al. 1989's
H-G phase-function coefficients), or cross-checked against two
independent secondary sources when the primary was paywalled
(Schulze-Makuch et al. 2011's ESI weights; Huber et al. 2011's solar
seismic reference values) -- flagged as weaker corroboration than a
primary read in each module's own docstring, not silently presented
as first-hand.

[PARTIAL] Habitability scoring (`habitability.py`, `habitability_eval.py`)
          Kopparapu et al. (2013 erratum) habitable-zone boundaries and
          Schulze-Makuch et al. (2011) Earth Similarity Index for a
          confirmed planet. `exoplanet_archive.py`'s `_PS_COLUMNS`/
          `PlanetRecord` extended with ten stellar/insolation/mass
          columns and a new `query_planets_bounded` catalog-scale query
          path (the original `query_confirmed_planets` single-host/
          single-planet guard is unchanged). Live-verified this session:
          `habitability.score_archive_planet("Kepler-10 b")` round-
          tripped through the real NASA Exoplanet Archive TAP service
          end to end via `rpc.dispatch`, confirming the ten new column
          names actually exist on the live `ps` table, not only in a
          mocked-response test.
          [GAP] The HZ is a 1D cloud-free radiative-convective result
          (Kasting-class model); the paper's own abstract states real
          boundaries "may extend further in both directions" once
          clouds are included. Eccentricity is reported on
          `PlanetRecord` but NOT integrated into a time-averaged flux
          (the paper's own Equation 4 extension is not implemented).
          ESI's surface term is built from equilibrium temperature, not
          a measured surface temperature -- no greenhouse model exists
          in this engine -- and the returned field is named
          `esi_surface_from_teq`, never `esi_surface`, to keep that
          substitution visible; a warning is always attached when it is
          used. Verified in `tests/test_habitability.py`: Earth scored
          via its true 288 K surface temperature reaches ESI=1.0
          exactly, but Earth scored via the actual code path (T_eq)
          does not.
          Tests: `tests/test_habitability.py` (22 cases) and
          `tests/test_habitability_eval.py` (9 cases, including
          reference-case recovery against the erratum's own Table 1
          Sun values and an HZ-membership-probability Monte Carlo with
          a Wilson interval). `tests/test_exoplanet_archive.py`'s
          existing 6 cases pass unchanged.

[PARTIAL] NEO hazard assessment (`neo_hazard.py`, `neo_hazard_eval.py`)
          MOID, Tisserand parameter, IAU H-G absolute magnitude/
          diameter, PHA classification, light-time correction, and
          close-approach distance, built entirely on top of the
          existing `moving_objects.py` (itself untouched -- imported,
          never edited). MOID is a numerical grid-search-plus-Nelder-
          Mead result, not Gronchi's (2005) algebraic method; its
          convergence is measured, not assumed (`neo_hazard_eval.
          moid_convergence` reports the error against exactly-known
          closed-form truth at five grid resolutions).
          [GAP] Two-body only: no planetary perturbations, no
          Yarkovsky effect, no Earth-Moon barycentre split, and --
          stated as the single most important line in the module --
          NO IMPACT PROBABILITY is ever computed, because that needs
          covariance propagation through a perturbed force model that
          does not exist here. `close_approach_sensitivity` in the eval
          module reports the SPREAD of close-approach distance under
          element-uncertainty resampling as an explicit sensitivity
          measure, never reframed as a probability. No JPL Horizons
          client; `moving_objects.mpc_search_orbits` remains the only
          live orbit lookup.
          Tests: `tests/test_neo_hazard.py` (34 cases, including exact
          closed-form MOID for coplanar circular orbits and identical
          orbits, and inclusive-boundary PHA classification at
          MOID=0.05 AU/H=22.0 exactly) and `tests/test_neo_hazard_eval.py`
          (9 cases).

[PARTIAL] Asteroseismology (`asteroseismology.py`, `asteroseismology_eval.py`)
          Kjeldsen & Bedding (1995) scaling relations with Huber et al.
          (2011) solar references (numax_sun=3090 uHz, Dnu_sun=135.1
          uHz), plus a pure-numpy detection path (Lomb-Scargle power
          spectrum -> Gaussian power-excess fit over a crude Harvey-
          profile background -> autocorrelation Dnu, disambiguated
          against the Stello et al. 2009 Dnu-numax relation) rather
          than depending on `lightkurve.seismology` for the
          implementation, even though `lightkurve` is already a core
          dependency -- kept as an optional API-surface cross-check only
          (`tests/test_asteroseismology_lightkurve.py`, no network
          needed), so the module stays offline-testable. That cross-check
          does NOT assert numerical agreement with this module's own
          estimator: a manual check found lightkurve's 2-D-autocorrelation
          numax estimator (Viani et al. 2019) disagreed substantially
          (~7145 uHz vs. an injected 1200 uHz truth) at default tuning on
          the same synthetic input -- different algorithms, different
          default assumptions, not evidence either is wrong, but a real
          finding worth recording rather than silently asserting
          agreement. Verified this session on a synthetic oscillation
          comb via this module's OWN estimator: recovered numax to
          within 0.2% and Dnu to within 0.01% of injected truth; solar
          round-trip inversion is exact to 1e-9.
          [GAP] No individual mode-frequency extraction, no
          peakbagging, no red-giant mixed-mode/period-spacing analysis
          (so no RGB-vs-red-clump classification), and no Sharma et al.
          (2016)-style correction to the Dnu scaling for evolved/metal-
          poor stars. The well-known x2/0.5x Dnu ACF-aliasing failure
          mode is not corrected for -- `asteroseismology_eval.
          measurement_recovery` MEASURES its rate (`n_aliased`) rather
          than assuming it away, the headline number of that study.
          Tests: `tests/test_asteroseismology.py` (16 cases) and
          `tests/test_asteroseismology_eval.py` (7 cases, including an
          exact algebraic round-trip check and the aliasing-rate
          measurement on real-cadence synthetic light curves).

[PARTIAL] Biosignature / transmission-spectrum retrieval
          (`biosignature.py`, `biosignature_fit.py`, `biosignature_eval.py`)
          Isothermal transmission-spectrum forward model (Lecavelier
          des Etangs et al. 2008) with Gaussian molecular-band
          templates (band centres/widths only -- peak cross sections
          are a REQUIRED caller input, never a module default) and a
          DE->Nelder-Mead->emcee fitter cloning `line_profile_fit.py`'s
          shape. A real numerics bug was found and fixed during manual
          verification before any test was written: the band model had
          no continuum-opacity floor, so `z = H*ln(sigma/sigma_ref)`
          blew up between bands from floating-point noise rather than
          real signal, producing wildly unstable fits; a fixed
          `CONTINUUM_OPACITY_FLOOR` constant resolved it, verified
          afterward by confirming flat spectra give a near-zero false-
          positive rate and strong injections are reliably detected.
          [GAP] -- the most important one in this whole feature pass:
          fitted band amplitudes are NOT calibrated absolute molecular
          abundances. There are no ExoMol/HITEMP line lists anywhere in
          this repository (petitRADTRANS-class radiative transfer was
          explicitly ruled out -- no new heavy dependency), so
          `detection_significance` reports a statistical band-detection
          significance (Delta BIC) only; a statement of the form "X ppm
          of water was detected" is not supported by this module. The
          `BiosignaturePanel.tsx` UI surfaces this as a persistent
          `<Note tone="bad">`, not only in the docstring, per explicit
          design requirement. `disequilibrium_flag`'s CH4+oxidant
          co-detection is a screening heuristic (Lovelock 1965), never
          a life detection -- abiotic false-positive pathways
          (Krissansen-Totton et al. 2018) are not evaluated. Also
          absent: multiple-scattering radiative transfer (isothermal/
          isobaric single-slab only), non-isothermal T-P profile, limb
          inhomogeneity, refraction, and stellar-contamination
          (unocculted spot/facula, Rackham et al. 2018) correction --
          likely the single largest real-world systematic in real
          transmission spectra, simply not modelled.
          Tests: `tests/test_biosignature.py` (20 cases), `tests/
          test_biosignature_fit.py` (10 cases), `tests/
          test_biosignature_eval.py` (8 cases, including the false-
          positive-rate calibration on pure-continuum spectra that is
          this module's headline metric).

[GAP] Technosignature (SETI) search (`technosignature.py`,
      `technosignature_eval.py`)
          Narrowband drift (de-Doppler) search on a caller-supplied
          `(n_time, n_freq)` dynamic spectrum. Brute-force shift-and-sum
          dedrift (Enriquez et al. 2017's +/-4 Hz/s, S/N>=10 defaults,
          confirmed against a secondary source quoting that paper's own
          search parameters), robust-baseline (1.4826*MAD) hit finding,
          and ON/OFF cadence RFI rejection (`cadence_filter` -- the
          single function that turns a hit list into a technosignature
          CANDIDATE list). Verified by hand before any test was written:
          zero-drift and both-sign drift injections recovered with the
          correct sign, pure noise gives zero false alarms across 20
          trials, and no wrap-around occurs at band edges. A real bug
          was found and fixed in `DynamicSpectrum`'s uniform-sampling
          check: a fixed-decimal-rounding uniqueness test spuriously
          rejected a perfectly uniform frequency grid at realistic
          ~1.4 GHz scale (float64 precision loss in the last few
          decimal digits at that magnitude), fixed with a relative-
          tolerance `np.allclose` check.
          [GAP] The O(N log N) Taylor-tree fast path (Taylor 1974) is
          DELIBERATELY NOT IMPLEMENTED -- this codebase's own standard
          is that a fast path must be checked bit-for-bit against a
          brute-force reference before being trusted, and the Taylor
          tree's edge-of-band combination rule could not be verified
          bit-exact in the time available. `dedrift_bruteforce` is
          therefore the only search path. No real Breakthrough Listen
          data ingestion: `h5py` is already a core dependency (`gw.py`
          uses it), so BL's HDF5 filterbank format is reachable in
          principle, but the exact `/data` layout and header-attribute
          schema has not been verified against a real file this
          session -- the same live-contract-gap class as the Chandra
          `packageset` gap above, not a shipped capability. SIGPROC
          `.fil` is not readable at all. Linear drift only (no
          acceleration term), incoherent summation only, single-dish
          only (no interferometric coincidence), and no signal
          classification -- a surviving hit is an unexplained
          narrowband detection, nothing more. Every number in
          `technosignature_eval.py` is measured on SYNTHETIC chi-
          squared-with-2-dof noise (the correct statistic for a
          square-law-detected spectrometer, not Gaussian); real data's
          RFI-dominated statistics are nothing like this, so the
          reported false-alarm rate is a floor for an idealised
          detector, not a real-world estimate, and `TechnosignaturePanel.
          tsx` states this in a persistent Note.
          Tests: `tests/test_technosignature.py` (20 cases, including
          explicit sign, band-edge-no-wrap, and cadence-filter RFI-
          rejection cases) and `tests/test_technosignature_eval.py`
          (9 cases, including a monotone-non-increasing false-alarm-
          vs-threshold check).

Full offline suite re-run after all five items landed: 2637 passed,
56 skipped, 0 failed. `npm run typecheck` and `cargo check
--manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml` both clean; the existing 70-test
vitest suite unaffected.
